### Description of the Binding

This image displays a highly ornate 17th-century red morocco <term>morocco: a high-quality goatskin leather known for its durability and ability to take gold-tooling</term> leather binding. The cover features extensive gold-tooling in the "fan" <note>original: "éventail"</note> style, a popular decorative technique of the Baroque period.

The decoration consists of multiple concentric rectangular borders filled with repeating lace-like and foliate <note>leaf-shaped</note> patterns. The central field is divided into compartments; the corners contain large quarter-circle fan designs composed of delicate <term>pointillé: a technique using small dots to create shimmering gold lines</term> gold work.

At the center of the design is an octagonal frame housing a coat of arms <note>heraldic symbols identifying the book's owner</note>. The shield features a sheaf of wheat <term>garb: a heraldic term for a bundle of grain</term> with two bands across it. The shield is surmounted by a crown held aloft by two winged <term>putti: chubby male infants, common in Renaissance and Baroque art</term>. A decorative floral element sits directly below the shield. On the far left edge, a small white library label is attached with the text "Vatican - Manuscripts".

<summary>This page describes a luxurious 17th-century red morocco leather binding from the Vatican Library, featuring intricate gold-tooled "fan" patterns and a central heraldic coat of arms supported by cherubs.</summary>
<keywords>Vatican Library, bookbinding, red morocco, gold-tooling, fan style, heraldry, 17th century, Baroque art</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page marks the beginning of a new section or treatise within the manuscript shelfmark Reg. lat. 1266, following a heavily damaged and illegible legal or administrative fragment. The tone shifts here to a formal, spiritual, and likely alchemical or philosophical invocation, typical of early modern German esoteric texts.</meta>

# In the Name of the Holy Trinity
## Of God the Father, and through the overflowing
## incomprehensible Wisdom of God the
## Son, and through the great
## infinite Love of the Ho-
## ly Spirit
->Amen!<-

---

From the time when God Almighty created Adam until this day, many highly learned men, authors, and writings have been dispensed through the grace of God the Holy Spirit. Just as the ancient pagan philosophers <note>original: "Heÿdnische Philosophi"</note> and thinkers well knew, and have left to us in their writings, no one alone is the source of the highest noble wisdom. We have witnesses here that they have written truly and in an orderly manner regarding the mystery <note>The word "Heimligkeit" refers to "arcana" or hidden secrets, often used in alchemical contexts to describe the internal workings of nature.</note> and wisdom of all things and arts which we possess. Yet no one may possess such things unless God grants them through a special understanding and knowledge of their origin and the writings of the ancient sages. It is not enough that one might have written well; rather, it is only through the sun of this wisdom <note>A common metaphor in Hermeticism for divine enlightenment or the "light of nature" that allows a seeker to understand hidden truths.</note>, with the requirement of long time and study of figures and allegories <note>original: "Figuren undt Gleichnußen"; these refer to the symbolic illustrations and parables used to hide and reveal esoteric knowledge.</note>, which are not to be cast aside.

<insert>
<image-desc>A round library stamp from the Vatican Library, bearing the text "VATICAN LIBRARY".</image-desc>
</insert>

<insert>
1266. Reg <note>Refers to the "Regina" collection, formerly belonging to Queen Christina of Sweden, now held in the Vatican.</note>
</insert>

<summary>This introductory page invokes the Holy Trinity to authorize a discourse on ancient wisdom and the "hidden secrets" of nature. It establishes that true knowledge comes from God and requires diligent study of the symbols and allegories left by ancient philosophers.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Trinity, Adam, Divine Wisdom, Pagan Philosophers, Arcana, Sun of Wisdom, Allegories, Esotericism, Alchemy</keywords>

...hitherto adding something, here in these <unclear>days</unclear> of tears until the time of revelation <unclear>through the</unclear> goodness<gloss>1</gloss> of God<gloss>2</gloss> the Almighty<gloss>3</gloss> which comes to us. The true<gloss>4</gloss> light<gloss>5</gloss> of Jesus Christ <note>A reference to the "Light of Nature" (Lumen Naturae), a central concept in Paracelsian and alchemical thought where divine truth is visible through both scripture and the natural world.</note>, which enlightens<gloss>7</gloss> every human being<gloss>6</gloss> who comes into this world, in which humans<gloss>8</gloss> God<gloss>9</gloss> the Almighty<gloss>10</gloss> has created the great mystery of all mysteries<gloss>11</gloss> within us<gloss>12</gloss>, His natural and graspable grace, an entirely<gloss>13</gloss> and<gloss>14</gloss> most-wise<gloss>15</gloss> hidden thing.

For<gloss>16</gloss> it is entirely possible for us<gloss>17</gloss> Christian people to understand all divine<gloss>18</gloss> mysteries<gloss>19</gloss> <note>original: "Heimligkeiten"; this term often refers to the hidden operations of God in nature or the secrets of the alchemical art.</note>, provided we are willing to direct<gloss>21</gloss> our understanding<gloss>20</gloss> toward them, and trust<gloss>22</gloss> in God and His grace; for He is patient<gloss>23</gloss> and ready with virtues, since He has created us<gloss>24</gloss> for this purpose<gloss>25</gloss>, so that every sinner should diligently<gloss>26</gloss> admonish<gloss>27</gloss> <note>In this context, "vermahnen" likely means to earnestly petition or remind God of His promises.</note> God, and at all times pray to and call upon Him for such grace<gloss>29</gloss> and wisdom of His<gloss>28</gloss>, for all wisdom<gloss>30</gloss> comes<gloss>31</gloss> from God<gloss>32</gloss> and is with Him without end; for such is the highest<gloss>33</gloss> and deepest<gloss>34</gloss> of all arts and sensibilities.

Thus, even if the ancient pagan masters<gloss>36</gloss> in schools<gloss>35</gloss> acknowledge <unclear>such</unclear><gloss>37</gloss> in their volumes<gloss>38</gloss> <note>original: "Bogen", literally "sheets" or "quires", referring to their written works.</note>, stating that they have revealed it to us in the most secret ways<gloss>39</gloss> <gloss>40</gloss>, we have communicated them here in human <unclear>limbs</unclear> <note>The word "liden" could refer to "limbs/members" or "sufferings"; given the context of communication, it likely refers to the human vessel or medium.</note> to those who demand<gloss>43</gloss> and desire<gloss>44</gloss> such art and wisdom<gloss>42</gloss> from God, each according<gloss>45</gloss> to his sensibility and understanding<gloss>46</gloss>. Therefore God<gloss>47</gloss> the Almighty<gloss>48</gloss> Father [turns] His divine<gloss>49</gloss> countenance<gloss>50</gloss> at all times upon us poor and needy<gloss>51</gloss>...

<summary>This page argues that divine wisdom and the "Mystery of Mysteries" are accessible to any Christian who seeks them with a focused mind and faith. It bridges the gap between ancient "pagan masters" (philosophers) and Christian revelation, framing the acquisition of knowledge as a spiritual duty.</summary>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory prayer and dedication, transitioning from the general possibility of understanding divine mysteries to a personal address to the Father and a specific introduction to the "Art" contained within the book.</meta>

My Holy Father, who extends mercy and justice to all ends of the earth, for He alone knows the thoughts of men and knows all things. Therefore, Almighty God, I thank You in Your Holy Trinity, for You demonstrate mercy and truth to mankind; for through Your grace in Your Tri-Unity, I speak of Your patience; in beauty, I acknowledge my wisdom; and in Your joy, I acknowledge Your goodness.

Thus You demonstrate Your works, so that we who laud and praise Them may read and compose this little book <note>original: "Büchlein"; a common humble term for a manuscript or treatise in this period.</note>. So shall my long promise be fulfilled, especially regarding what would turn out well; for otherwise, I would have written these words in vain. It is also intended that everything be learned from it to preserve us when judged in misfortune. For there are many hidden names in these letters <note>This likely refers to "Kabbalistic" or mystical names of God and angels believed to be encoded in the text.</note>; and furthermore, the truth of this Art <note>The "Art" (Kunst) almost certainly refers to Alchemy or the Hermetic science of transformation.</note>—which Almighty God has bestowed, giving perfection of wisdom—which You possess in the heights of Heaven, You who create and govern all things.

You are a flowing fountain <term>Bron (Fountain/Spring)</term> and the gatekeeper of eternal wisdom, and the Word of the most high Divine Hierarchy <note>original: "Gottlicke Hierarki"; a reference to the celestial order of angels and divine emanations.</note>, the essence of all things. Therefore, in the beginning, those who wish to learn the Art and science should possess the treasure of understanding, which consists of God’s gathering in this book, of which we alone are worthy.

<summary>This page concludes the author's prayerful dedication to the Trinity, framing the manuscript as a divinely-inspired "Art" containing hidden names and wisdom for the worthy seeker.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Trinity, Divine Hierarchy, Hidden Names, The Art, Wisdom, Eternal Fountain, Understanding</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general invocation of the previous page to the specific structure of the treatise. It introduces the "six chapters" that form the body of the work and establishes the "foundation of prayer" as the primary requirement for the student of this Art.</meta>

The spiritual Word, which is the Word of God <note>original: "Geistigkeite Wort, welches Wort Gottes"; the author suggests that the text following is an extension of divine logos or revelation.</note>, shall also be brought down here from the Word of the truest victory of the book with great diligence, out of the deepest wonder, into the garden of the greatest assembly of the true planting of the perfection of all sensibilities <term>Sinnligkeiten (Sensibilities/Perceptions)</term> <note>In this mystical context, this refers to the spiritual senses or the human faculty for perceiving divine truths within the natural world.</note>—everything that God distributes with His creation and all His created things and creatures throughout nature.

And this is now, and shall be, divided into six special chapters so that those things may be presented for study in the most useful, actual progression of things, which are described and taught by the highest providence <note>original: "Vorkommenheit"; while it literally means "occurrence," in this period's theological German, it often serves as a variant for "Vollkommenheit" (perfection) or "Vorsehung" (providence).</note>. And the Master who desires to see the inner, hidden sensibilities in this Art <term>Kunst (Art)</term> <note>As noted previously, "The Art" is a standard term for Alchemy, Hermeticism, or the "Great Work" of spiritual transformation.</note> should study the instructions named hereafter in the name of the Lord, the Savior Jesus Christ.

He will certainly and surely find the doctrine of the sensibilities arising with the help of God’s grace and according to the perception of his understanding—how great it is—and we see its branches through his assembly. Thus the conclusion of the foundation of the prayer is laid down; which foundation of prayer is richer than all wisdom, the world of nature, and fortune—which is dearer and more precious than all silver and gold, gemstones and jewels that are upon the earth,

<meta>catchword:</meta> Which which

<summary>This page introduces the structural organization of the book into six chapters and emphasizes that the "Art" (alchemy or mysticism) is rooted in the "Word of God" and the "foundation of prayer." It frames the study of nature and spiritual perception as a path more valuable than any material wealth.</summary>

<keywords>Word of God, Six Chapters, Sensibilities, Sacred Art, Jesus Christ, Foundation of Prayer, Nature, Alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion of the "Art" and its chapters. The fragment from the previous page ("its bran...") likely concludes with a reference to the branching of divine knowledge or perception. This page transitions into a critique of worldly scholarship versus true spiritual insight.</meta>

...branching <note>The previous page ended with "its bran...", likely completing the word "branching" or "branches," referring to the unfolding of divine understanding.</note>.

Secondly, regarding the matter of study, the path is not prepared for the blessed explanation of those circles <term>Zirckel (Circles)</term> <note>In mystical cosmology, these "circles" often refer to the celestial spheres or the cyclic diagrams used to illustrate the relationship between the divine and the material world.</note> which lead to the throne and state of the <unclear>highest</unclear> heavenly song and the hidden science; for while the light—along with that which allows itself to be darkened—does not venture upon it, it is incomprehensible to entrust it even halfway. I say this as clearly as it is of God: they <note>Likely referring to worldly scholars or those without spiritual illumination.</note> have all their lives not known us in the architecture of the secret mystery <term>Heimligkeit (Secret Mystery)</term> and have never in their lives recognized such things within it.

Therefore, Lord, it is just as with the poor man who goes blind to the Last Supper <term>Abendmahl (Last Supper/Communion)</term> and does not know how the food has been prepared; and thus they say much and yet study much of things for which they have no understanding in the truth. So it happens to them according to their will: they are [agitated] here with the movement <note>original: "Bewegung"; in the mystical tradition of Jacob Boehme, "movement" often refers to the restless, circling quality of the material soul or the "wheel of nature" before it finds divine rest.</note>.

Therefore, be certain of this: those who wish to be clever *per se* <note>original: "per se"; Latin for "by itself" or "on its own."</note> with highly learned loudmouths <note>original: "Dickmäulern"; literally "fat-mouths," a derisive term for pompous academics or theologians who possess head-knowledge but no heart-knowledge.</note> argue about many things just as the blind man speaks of color. Therefore, you who study, know the virtuous powers which come from here, from the Father of the secret mystery. This mystery is beyond all sensibility of the inner nature <term>Beschaffenheit (Nature/Constitution)</term> <note>This refers to the essential quality or spiritual "make-up" of a thing.</note> which is hidden in all things of the creatures. Within the creatures, it <note>The divine gift.</note> is uncreated and works as the gift of God in the name of our Lord

> Jesus Christ. Amen. It has no beginning nor circumference <note>original: "Umfang"; here suggesting that the divine essence is infinite and cannot be bounded or measured.</note>.

<summary>The author warns that true spiritual knowledge cannot be reached through worldly study or academic pride, comparing unilluminated scholars to blind men at a feast. He emphasizes that the "secret mystery" is an uncreated gift of God hidden within all creatures.</summary>

<keywords>circles, heavenly song, hidden science, secret mystery, communion, loudmouths, uncreated gift, Jesus Christ, infinity</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the theological argument regarding the nature of the Divine. The previous page ended with "Theref...", which likely concluded a thought on why worldly scholars fail to grasp the "secret mystery." This page begins mid-sentence, establishing the eternal nature of God as being without beginning or end.</meta>

...had and shall also have no end; that is unavoidable. Together with believing that God had no beginning nor <note>original: "Anfang"; the author distinguishes between "Beginn" and "Anfang," though both roughly translate to beginning or starting point.</note> starting point; for had God had a beginning, then that same beginning must be a substance or essence <term>Wesen (Essence/Being)</term> <note>In 17th-century German mysticism, "Wesen" refers not just to a thing's nature, but its actual spiritual reality or "beingness."</note> so that such an essence would al<gloss>h</gloss>ways <note>The scribe added an 'H' between the letters of the word "immer" (always).</note> have had a beginning, and that same beginning must then also have had a beginning, and so forth until neither end nor beginning could ever be found. Conversely, if such an understanding <note>original: "Verständ"; refers here to a logical grasp or conceptualization of a beginning.</note> could have found a beginning—since we see so clearly that when one can find the beginning of a thing, then that same thing is not an eternal beginning, and thus not the origin and cause of all things.

<margin>Therefore</margin> since one can find no beginning, it must therefore result purely in what one finds hereafter as beginning and starting point; if indeed His substance were without beginning and starting point, then that same substance must therewith have been in its "first being," from which all other things have come. Therewith it is unavoidable to believe that God has had no beginning, for no beginning nor starting point is found in God; and thus it is natural to confess that He also may take no end. For where any essence or substance of a thing has a beginning, through that same essence it may be turned to dust <note>original: "Zer- staubt"; literally "to be dusted" or "pulverized," meaning to dissolve back into nothingness.</note> again and have an end. God [is] of no essence nor substance [that had a] beginning or starting point; therefore He can have neither end nor...

<summary>This page presents a logical and mystical proof for the eternity of God, arguing that if God had a beginning, it would require an infinite regress of causes. It concludes that because God is the unoriginated "first being," He is also immune to dissolution or end.</summary>
<keywords>eternity, substance, essence, first being, infinite regress, cosmology, divine nature</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>The previous page established that God has no beginning or starting point, arguing that if a beginning could be found, God would not be eternal or the cause of all things. This page continues that thought, transitioning from God’s lack of a beginning to how He exists within Himself and reveals His essence to the world.</meta>

...is natural to confess that He is, from then on, a contemplation <note>original: "Betrachtung"</note> of how God is in Himself, and whether God was alone without beginning; for if a beginning were found where God worked at the start, why should He also remain eternal? And so God was alone; His starting point <note>original: "Anfangspunkt"</note> shall also be eternal: without end, and His power is everywhere in all ends, inside all things. 

And God in Himself is His eternal essence <term>Wesen (Essence/Being)</term> <note>In this mystical context, "Wesen" describes the actual spiritual substance of the Divine.</note> in which there were the forms of all His properties: of His Wisdom (the Son) and in the will of His Holy Spirit. This shape and radiance <term>Schein (Radiance/Appearance)</term> <note>The author uses "Schein" not to mean "fake," but to describe the outward shine or manifestation of an inner light.</note> was in Him: God the Son in His Godhead. Yet God can well be hidden from the world where He wills, and what He wills, that He can also always reveal. 

Nevertheless, His hiddenness and His openness are not two different things; for He did not hide Himself too long but opened Himself according to His divine will. This was hidden at first, or like an emerging radiance, the nature of which is not graspable by human knowledge through their understanding or their senses. This form, shape, and radiance of the Godhead is within His divine essence, which was given forth and appeared for glory—having been a reflection <note>original: "Spiegel-sein"; literally "mirror-being," referring to the divine mirror in which God views His own reflection.</note> and a radiance, just as the spirits have given recognition of perfection and so naturally stood.

<summary>This page explores the internal nature of the Trinity—Wisdom as the Son and Will as the Spirit—and how God’s hidden essence is revealed as a "radiance" or "reflection" that transcends human sensory understanding.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Wisdom of the Son, Holy Spirit, divine essence, radiance, hiddenness, revelation, eternity</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded with the idea that God's hiddenness and His revelation are not separate, but rather a manifestation of His divine will and essence, like a reflection in a mirror. This page continues that exploration of divine light and the nature of God's substance, specifically how the "hidden light" interacts with human perception and the physical world.</meta>

For a white, clear color contains much light, as well as the extraordinarily hidden <note>original: "eußer-bergener"; likely a combination of "außer" (beyond) and "verborgen" (hidden), referring to colors beyond human perception.</note> and indescribable colors within it. Indeed, every clear color is so lovely and pleasant to behold because of the radiance of the shine within it, and because of the light shining forth and through the appearance of those hidden colors. 

This radiance would cause pain to mortal eyes and would cause human sight to bow before its shine; the mere glint of that hidden, fiery light—which cannot be named—leads the sight into a finer, hidden darkness <note>This refers to the "Divine Darkness," a mystical concept where the light of God is so intense that it transcends human vision, appearing as darkness to the physical eye.</note>. Therefore, the Spirit startles the eyes of the flesh, because they cannot see it; for this [sight] exists only in clear faith.

Yet this light cannot be joined with any created beings, but only with God: Jesus: who signifies the Word <note>A reference to the *Logos*, the Divine Word through which the world was created (John 1:1).</note>. This remains unknown; and light, through an inner sight, precedes all of God's powerful acts in heaven and earth. Therefore, God is a Body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> with a Spirit, for He is conceived spiritually, just as fire is in the outer flame. For fire is considered to be half-Body and half-Spirit <note>original: "Spiritus"</note>. This is because that which one sees is the body, and that which one cannot grasp is spiritual. Therefore it is said: God is a fire and a spirit, and the Spirit of God is conceived in His eternal essence <term>Wesen (Essence/Being)</term>, which essence is thought of as the Light and Blessedness of all light—which is established there as a spiritual Body, a powerful essence.

<summary>This page describes the nature of divine light as something that contains hidden colors and is too intense for physical eyes, requiring "inner sight" or faith. It explains God's nature using the metaphor of fire, characterizing Him as both a spiritual Body (Corpus) and a Spirit (Spiritus).</summary>
<keywords>divine light, hidden colors, spiritual body, Jesus as the Word, fire metaphor, essence, spiritual vision</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>The previous page discussed the nature of divine light and the concept of God as a "Body with a Spirit," comparing the divine essence to the relationship between fire and its flame. Invisibility is the power and the life by which all things take their character, live, grow, and multiply; and in this essence, all imperishable and incorruptible invisible creatures exist and remain. ## The Second Working of God.

In the second working, God spoke: let the firmament be in the midst of the waters, to divide those that were below the firmament from those that were above the firmament. And God called the firmament Heaven. This Heaven was made of the most noble, pure, subtle, and highest of the most perfect substance.

<meta>This page transitions from the discussion of the "fifth essence" (the pure, celestial substance) to a description of the Creation, specifically the third day, to explain the nature and hierarchy of the four base elements—Earth, Water, Air, and Fire—from which all earthly things are composed.</meta>

## The Third Working of God.

In the third working, God spoke: let the waters that are under the heaven be gathered into one place, and let the dry land appear; and God called the dry land the Earth, and the gathering of the waters he called the Sea.

In this third working of God, the four <term>elements</term> <note>The basic building blocks of matter in ancient and medieval science: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire.</note> <gloss>1</gloss> were perfected, each in its own essence, distinct <gloss>2</gloss> and essential within itself. Among these elements, three possess visibility: <gloss>2</gloss> namely <gloss>2</gloss> Earth, Water, and Fire. The <gloss>2</gloss> fourth possesses <gloss>2</gloss> invisibility, which is the Air. All four of these are perfectly suited to all unstable, <term>perishable</term> <note>original: "verwesentlichen"; referring to things that can rot or decay, unlike the eternal heavens.</note> creatures and lowly beings. Among the three visible elements, there are no more than two <term>corporeal</term> <note>original: "Leichnamliche oder Corperliche"; meaning they have a physical "body" that occupies space.</note> or bodily elements—specifically Earth and Water—which the human hand can grasp. For every thing that one can see and touch is a body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and an earthly substance.

The two other elements are spiritual elements, such as Fire and Air, which man cannot grasp, though one certainly feels them. And Fire is no more than a "half-spirit" <gloss>2</gloss> because it can be seen; yet, because it cannot be grasped, it is a spirit <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> and is a soul and the highest element. For without the element of Fire, one cannot live without its power and its spiritual essence.

## The Third Power

Through the Word that God spoke: "Let the waters bring forth creeping things and a living soul, and the birds upon the earth under the firmament of heaven; and let the earth bring forth a living soul in its form: beasts and creeping animals of the earth, each according to its form." And He created the same for all animals—which now [live] wrongly without any recognition [of their origin]. This is shown to us for the sake of the divine subtlety <note>original: "Subtilitat"; refers to the refined, spiritualized, or intricate nature of God's handiwork in the elements.</note> of the natures of the Air, which are warm and moist. These airy elements are not merely taken up by the animals; nor is the lowliness of their earthly bodies [accidental], for God the Almighty has thus composed this <term>complexion</term> <note>original: "Complexion"; the specific balance of the four primary qualities—hot, cold, moist, and dry—that determines the physical and spiritual temperament of a being.</note> of the natures. And in these two qualities—warmth and moisture—He placed the nature of life, through which all things live and exist in their essence. All things act and grow, for without heat there is no reality <note>original: "würcklichkeit"; literally "work-ability," meaning the active force or efficacy that allows a thing to function and exist in a tangible state.</note> in either perfect or imperfect things. And if warmth and moisture depart from things or beings, then that same thing dies and passes away. # The Fourth Work of God

In this work God spoke: "Let there be light in the firmament of heaven to divide the day and the night, and let them be signs, times, days, and years, that they shine in the firmament of heaven and illuminate the earth." In this fourth work, God created the heavenly bodies from the firmament of the <term>fifth essence</term> <note>original: "quinta essentia"; the purest, incorruptible substance that makes up the celestial spheres, distinct from the four earthly elements.</note> of heaven. These bodies are the stars of the twelve signs <note>The twelve signs of the Zodiac.</note> of heaven and other stars and the planets; all of these are finely purified <term>fifth essence</term>. And the firmament of heaven divides itself into various distinct parts, which parts are enclosed one over another, like a yolk in an egg <note>A common medieval and Renaissance metaphor for the geocentric model of the universe, where the Earth is the center (the yolk) surrounded by concentric layers of celestial spheres.</note>. Into these nine heavens or distinct parts, God the Almighty set the signs, stars, and planets, and the two great lights. And the great light (which among the two great lights is there) is the Sun, which should rule the day; and the smallest light of the two great lights, which is the Moon, which should rule the night; and [ordained] that they should shine upon the Earth and govern the day and night. Thus God adorned the heaven with the heavenly bodies and stars, the planets, and with the two great lights, which shine so beautifully day and night, being much brighter and clearer than the heaven; this is according to the unity...

# The 5th Work of God.

In this fifth work, God spoke: let the waters bring forth creeping things, which are living souls, and birds upon the earth under the <term>firmament</term> <note>original: "Firmament"; the vault of the heavens which, in the Genesis account, separates the waters above from the waters below.</note> of heaven. In this fifth work, God created great whales and to each a living, moving soul in the waters, so that each brings forth its own kind and form; and He created the birds from the airy elements, each to bring forth its own kind and form. And God blessed <note>original: "benedeyet"; from the Latin *benedicere*.</note> them and said: increase and multiply and fill the waters of the sea, and you birds shall be multiplied upon the earth. Thus has God Almighty endowed and adorned the water with the moving, living things—the fish which are in the waters—and has endowed and adorned the air and earth with the living things of the birds and all animals upon earth. God has created all of these out of the great, longing love He has for mankind, so that the life of man could be sustained out of and through the <term>complexion</term> <note>original: "Complexion"; in medieval and early modern thought, this refers to the specific balance of the four elements (fire, air, water, earth) or humors within a living being that determines its health and nature.</note> of the four lovely elemental natures; so that one living thing must sustain the other, because they are a desire born of the living essence, just as they are of the material essence.

# The Sixth Work of God

In this sixth work, God spoke: "Let the earth bring forth a living soul according to its kind: beasts and creeping things among the animals of the earth, each according to its kind and form upon the earth." And so Almighty God endowed and adorned the earth with living animals, from the lions and the awesome creatures of the green earth, to the animals such as the foxes, <unclear>stags</unclear>, and other multitudes of earthly forms. God spoke: "Let us make a human after our image, and he shall rule over the fish in the sea and over the birds under the heavens and over the animals of the earth and all creeping animals that are brought to life on earth." And Almighty God created the human from the mud of the earth; which earth was made of eight pieces or substances. This mud and earth was an extract of the <term>substantiality</term> <note>original: "Substialitet"; refers to the essential, underlying matter or the most refined part of a physical substance.</note> of the four elements—from the four elements of the earth—and this <term>subtlety</term> <note>original: "Subtieligkeit"; in an alchemical or theological context, this refers to the most refined, non-gross essence of a material.</note> was composed of seven substances.

# The Multiplication of the Philosophers' Stone
## is to be understood in the form as follows.

The ancients, through the enlightenment of the Holy Spirit, held that
Almighty God created a single nature with substance,
thoroughly perfect inside and out for His praise,
and how all things in the beginning sprang from one root,
how God created nature, how one nature
was connected with another, how one nature
became out of another, and how, with great order, one nature
transforms into another and maintains itself within the other. <gloss>1</gloss> As an example:
1. 2. 3. 4. <gloss>2</gloss> from which comes 10. 20. 30. 40. <gloss>3</gloss> from which come 100.
200. 300. 400. <gloss>4</gloss> from which come 1000. 2000. 3000. 4000. and
thus all these numbers come from 1. 2. 3. 4. You have **demonstrative figures of the multiplications of the stone** <note>original: "figuras demonstrativas multiplicationum lapidis"; these are likely diagrams or charts showing how the power of the Stone increases exponentially.</note> before you; for just as the
number grows from the first number and becomes a higher and
increasing number, from which alone all other numbers come,
both by the composition of the numbers and then through the
**arranged zeros** <note>original: "nulla ordinari"; refers to the mathematical practice of adding zeros to increase the value of a digit by powers of ten.</note>, they become 100. 200. 300. 400. Likewise, our Art
is entirely a single substance and root, the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "☿ Philosophorum"; this is not common quicksilver, but the "First Matter" or essential spirit of the metals.</note>,
from which a work shall immediately [come] through **dissolution** <note>original: "Solution"</note>; for
it is dissolved into water, air, and fire. Although
they have become [many] from one number, yet that same spirit is
like a mustard seed <note>A biblical reference to something small that contains a great kingdom or power within it.</note>, which is threefold and yet is only one, and the one
has been hidden in the <gloss>2</gloss> three [and] has been <unclear>seen</unclear>. Yet now the

<summary>This page explains the alchemical concept of multiplication by comparing it to the way single digits (1-4) can be increased to thousands by adding zeros. It identifies the "Mercury of the Philosophers" as the single root of the Great Work, which contains a hidden trinity within its unity.</summary>
<keywords>Multiplication, Philosophers' Stone, Mercury of the Philosophers, dissolution, numerical symbolism, mustard seed, unity and trinity</keywords>

--- Page 86 ---
<meta>This page continues the numerical analogy of the "Multiplication" of the Stone introduced on the previous page. While the former page established the basic progression from 1 to 40, this page explains how that progression represents the exponential increase of the Stone’s transmuting power through "grading" and the mathematical addition of "zeros."</meta>

# The Multiplication of the Stone

The multiplication of the stone is nothing other than a distribution of the single thing from the first, lowest degree up to the very highest. Therefore, it follows that when the number one from the first line is placed upon it, it becomes ten. And thus our multiplication after the solution is completed <note>original: "multiplicatio post factam solutionem"; in alchemy, "solution" refers to the process of dissolving the solid matter into a liquid state so it can be purified and increased.</note> is taken from the first line and placed into the next weight and increased, so that the quality and power of the matter may immediately <term>tinge</term> <note>to "tinge" means to transform or transmute a base metal into gold or silver through the application of the Stone.</note> ten pounds. However, because it should not be removed until it has become perfect <note>original: "perfect"</note> and potent in the first distribution—and one part is again held threefold, each growing even higher until the other division yields four lines—it is then so high that it cannot be brought any higher. Thus, the multiplication <note>original: "multiplicatio"</note> and the power to tinge happens at the lower and highest degrees; so from the one comes 100, then 1000. The last number is the highest from the first. Therefore, one part of the <term>perfect stone</term> <note>Latin: "lapidis perfect"; the completed Philosophers' Stone at its ultimate stage of development.</note> also has the power to <term>tinge</term> 100 parts, for this has occurred through the measurement of nature, from the lowest degree to the highest. And just as one counts 10, and from 10 comes 20, from 20 comes 30, from 30 comes 40—so too with this number, where through experience another zero <note>original: "nulla"; the text uses the mathematical concept of adding a zero to increase a value tenfold as a metaphor for alchemical refinement.</note> is added, or an increase, or through a <term>grading</term> <note>a "grading" or "graduation" is a process used to increase the potency, color, or purity of an alchemical substance.</note> occurs. Thus, from 10 comes 100, from 20 comes 200, from 30 comes 300, and from 40 comes 400.

<summary>This page explains the "Multiplication" of the Philosophers' Stone, using a decimal mathematical analogy to describe how the substance's power to transmute base metals increases exponentially through successive refinements.</summary>
<keywords>multiplication, transmutation, tinging, grading, numerical analogy, perfect stone, solution, decimal progression</keywords>

# THE ILLUSTRIOUS KING
<note>original Latin: "INCLYTI REGIS"</note>
### <unclear>Multiplication of the Philo[sophers] in Succession in Clarity</unclear>
<note>original: "Multiplicatio Philo... in Successione in der Claritæt"</note>

So that now the fourth <gloss>1</gloss> and final <term>elevation</term> <note>An "elevation" (or exaltation) is an alchemical process that raises a substance to a higher state of purity and power, often through repeated distillation or refinement.</note> has occurred, from 100 becomes one thousand <gloss>2</gloss>, and 200 becomes 2,000 <gloss>3</gloss>, and from 300 become 3,000 <gloss>4</gloss>, and from 400 become 4,000 <gloss>5</gloss>. And thus the <term>matter</term> <note>original: "materia"; the substance being worked upon in the alchemical vessel.</note> multiplies itself through a single elevation; and though nothing is added to the physical volume of the matter, adding only a <term>zero</term> <note>original: "nulla"; the author notes that while a zero represents "nothing" in counting, in alchemical mathematics it represents a tenfold increase in the Stone's strength.</note>—which is no number in itself—nevertheless it indicates the great and manifold significance of the power.

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page transitions from the previous illegible leaf to a formal dedication. While the earlier parts of the manuscript (around page 59) discussed the philosophical stone, this page serves as a formal address to John Dee’s patron, likely King Maximilian II.</meta>

## TO THE ILLUSTRIOUS KING
# MAXIMILIAN’S MOST EXCELLENT
# MAJESTY.
### John Dee of London
-> Desires for him a happy reign. <note>original Latin: "Imperium optat felicis"</note> <-

The two reasons which have prompted me—as one of humble status—to honor such a high and mighty King with this present, modest little gift <note>original: "Beschencklein"; Dee uses this diminutive to express humility before the monarch</note> from my heart, I here set forth, delivering them alongside this work <note>Dee refers to the manuscript itself as the "work" or "gift"</note>. Namely, first: my especially inclined and most humble love and favor which I bear toward Your Majesty. Second: the gift itself, which, although it appears to be of small account, is nonetheless something unusual and quite rare in this world; furthermore, it is a gift which, on account of its inherent value, is by no means to be despised.

---

However, that I bear such a particularly inclined will toward Your Majesty is caused by your excellent and high virtues, which are so elevated and renowned <note>original: "veruhrnumbt," an archaic form of "vornehm" or "berühmt," meaning distinguished or famous</note> that even those who are not permitted to behold them with their own eyes <insert>cannot help but recognize them</insert>...

<summary>John Dee formally dedicates his work to King Maximilian II, citing his humble devotion and the rare, valuable nature of the alchemical knowledge he is presenting as his primary motivations.</summary>

<keywords>John Dee, Maximilian II, dedication, royal patronage, alchemy, virtue, humble gift</keywords>

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page continues John Dee’s dedication to King Maximilian II. Following the initial praise of the King’s virtues, Dee now validates these claims based on his own personal observations during his travels to Hungary, transitioning into a philosophical reflection on the stages of human life and the difficulty of explaining his complex alchemical ideas simply.</meta>

...others spoke of it by giving credit to hearsay, with the custom that they claim to hold high virtues within themselves [surpassing] others eternally, and speak the truth; but those who have seen such things quite personally <note>original: "gegenworthiglich," meaning to be physically present as an eyewitness</note>, and have afterward considered them with diligence, they will be forced to freely confess that [these virtues] belong to Your Majesty according to both speech and writing. They must be sought after and possessed, for they belong to all speech and words, and yet [ordinary words] must be avoided [as inadequate]. And that this is true, I experienced sufficiently and in manifold ways this past autumn, when I stayed for a time in the Kingdom of **Hungary** at **Pressburg** <note>modern-day Bratislava; Dee traveled there in September 1563 for Maximilian’s coronation as King of Hungary</note>. I also saw it with my own eyes and recognized it.

But as to why I should boast of and profit from this undertaking—which appears so slight—as something unusual in the world, while I have considered with all diligence how I might most conveniently explain and make this understood with but a few short words: it occurs to my mind how the course <term>course</term> <note>original: "Lauff," referring to the natural progression or flow of a person's life stages</note> of human life is conducted, and how every person tends to hold differing opinions; and just as one opinion is held and used by the great majority, such a manner and way cannot be [easily] understood and considered. <unclear>Moreover</unclear>, then, when a person has passed the course of their childhood and [emerged] from their youth...

<summary>Dee validates his praise of King Maximilian II by citing his personal observations in Pressburg and begins a philosophical reflection on the "course of human life" to explain the difficulty of conveying his rare knowledge.</summary>
<keywords>Hungary, Pressburg, eyewitness testimony, royal virtue, course of life, childhood, John Dee, Maximilian II</keywords>

--- Page 91 ---
<meta>Continuity: On the previous page, John Dee established his credibility as an eyewitness to the virtues of King Maximilian II during his 1563 visit to Hungary. He began a philosophical digression on the various paths human lives take and the difficulty of explaining his profound ideas to those with differing motivations.</meta>

# THE ILLUSTRIOUS KING’S
<note>original Latin: "INCLYTI REGIS"</note>

I would have written into the album <note>original: "Alben," referring to an "album amicorum" or book of friends—a popular 16th-century custom where noblemen collected signatures and mottos</note> of a young man of noble lineage: as soon as his mind and spirit <term>spirit</term> <note>original: "Gemüth," referring to the seat of a person's thoughts, emotions, and moral intent</note> began to reflect upon in what station of life he intended and desired to spend his time from then on, until the spirit [decided] which path it preferred to enter and walk upon; finally, upon sudden reflection <note>original: "Schlags Bedenckens," a sudden striking thought or realization</note>, when he is won over through <unclear>mad</unclear> worldly favor toward virtue, purity, and truth, so that he will direct and exert all his ability toward how he might spend his entire life in **Philosophy** and the good arts; or, on the other hand, should he allow himself to be more beguiled by worldly transience <term>transience</term> <note>original: "Vergängligkeit," the fleeting nature of material existence</note>, and be moved by fluttering desires toward the fortune of war, so that he thinks and strives toward how he might attain and lead his temporal and glorious life. And because of this manner or resolution of his noble understanding—whereas by contrast, among those who wish to concern themselves with **Philosophy**, hardly one would be satisfied of whom it could be said with truth that he had tasted even the very first beginning of the foundation upon which true **Physics** <term>Physics</term> <note>original: "Physica," here referring to the study of the natural world and its hidden laws or "natural philosophy"</note> is established. But he who considers more perfectly the causes of the heavenly powers and their effects, along with the origin, essence, and destruction of other things, he has with pleas—

<vocab>Philosophy, Physics, Spirit, Album, Fortune of War, Transience</vocab>

<summary>Dee uses the metaphor of a young nobleman choosing his life's path to contrast a life of worldly military glory with a life of philosophical study. He argues that true Natural Philosophy (Physics) requires a deep understanding of celestial powers and the essence of matter that few actually possess.</summary>
<keywords>John Dee, Philosophy, Physics, life choices, military glory, natural laws, noble education, transience</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>Continuity: On the previous page, the author discussed the difficulty of finding individuals dedicated to true philosophy and the "foundation of Physics." This page continues that thought, emphasizing the extreme rarity of such mastery and introducing the use of the "Hieroglyphic Type" (the Monas symbol) to explain higher secrets to the Emperor.</meta>

Among those who devote themselves entirely to such a
study of wisdom, there will hardly be one in thousands
to show or represent this; and what is more,
how could we hope that in these appointed times
a common man of the world [could achieve] a single such work,
which might soon be encountered—one who, after
having overcome and endured the two things indicated,
should never have arrived at the right **Speculation** <term>Speculation</term> <note>original: "Speculation"; in this context, it refers to deep philosophical or mystical contemplation of the divine order</note> and
understanding of the super-celestial powers and supernatural
phenomena; although according to the progress <note>original Latin: "progressu"</note>

<meta>This page continues the author’s address to the King, transitioning from a discussion of Jewish Kabbalistic techniques (Temurah, Notarikon, Tzeruf) to his own specialized definition of "Real Kabbalah." ...is inclined and accustomed to doing good not only to the Jews, but also to all teachers and peoples of all nations and languages without respect of person. This is because the royal treasury <note>original: "Kammergut." I have called these the **Essential Kabbalah** <note>original: "wesentliche cabalam."</note> in our **Aphorisms** <note>original: "Aphorismis." I call the other common type **Grammatical Kabbalah** <note>original: "cabalam Grammaticam"</note> or **Verbal Kabbalah** <note>original: "Verbalem,"</note> which deals with words and the flaws in the familiar letters as they are written by men.

But that Kabbalah which is innate to us according to the law of creation—as Paul also testifies—is specifically the **Real Kabbalah** <note>original: "Cabala Realis." A form of mysticism based on the actual structure of the physical and spiritual world rather than just the study of words.</note>. It is somewhat higher and holier than the Grammatical Kabbalah, because it is an inventor of new arts and a faithful interpreter and explainer of the most secret, hidden things, just as others will be able to understand more fully by following our example. 

Whether I now, **Most Gracious King** <note>original: "Gnädigster König."</note>, venture to present this **Magical Parable** <note>original: "MAGICAM parabolam"</note> or likeness in Your Majesty's presence, I know well that Your Majesty will have no fear of it, because our **Hieroglyphic Monad** <note>original: "Monas Hieroglyphica." A complex occult symbol created by John Dee meant to represent the mystical unity of all creation.</note> expresses and keeps hidden within itself...

Thus, just as it is unavoidable that the ill-will of an envious heart shall result in such dishonor, shame, and untruthful slanders <note>original: "Calumnien," referring to malicious misrepresentations of his work.</note> being cast upon him; so too will all the most disciplined, humble, and wisest philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> acknowledge—those who have for many cruel years burdened themselves alongside me—all the praise that is due. denselben **substances** <term>bodies</term> <note>original: "Corporum"</note> kommen und einnehmen welche gar fein
künstlich etliche **shadowy figures** <term>symbolic forms</term> <note>original: "umbratiles figuras"</note> gebraucht und zugelaßen
Auf das den groben Eseln die in die schönen lieblichen Gärten
der **Hesperides** <term>nymphs of the sunset</term> <note>original: "Hesperidum"; in mythology, they guarded a garden of golden apples; here used as a metaphor for secret knowledge</note> mit grober unverschaumbheit einfallen, die aller-
außerleßensten **lettuce** <term>choice greens</term> <note>original: "Lactuca"; used here metaphorically for refined food/knowledge</note> nicht zu theil werden, da man Sie wohl

<summary>The author uses the metaphor of the Garden of the Hesperides to argue that true "substance" should be reserved for the wise, while the "coarse" and ignorant should only be permitted to see "shadows" and "thistles." He appeals to the King to discern between these two classes of people and justifies his harsh criticism of the world's injustice by comparing his mission to that of Christ.</summary>

<keywords>philosophy, shadows and substance, Hesperides, justice, social hierarchy of knowledge, Platonic thought, royal judgment</keywords>

--- Page 115 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's dedication to a royal patron, shifting from a philosophical defense of "substances" over "shadows" to a personal account of the work's creation. He presents the following text as a "fruit" of his long-standing wisdom, now brought to light through divine and "magnetic" influence.</meta>

<page-num>53.</page-num>

# SOLOMON'S HIEROGLYPHICS

Especially what I have experienced in inner **wisdom** <note>original: "Weisheit"</note>, I mean to express here outwardly—yet indeed not as though it should be considered too much or superfluous. Let this be said up to this point:

I hereby offer, in all humility, this my **fruit** <note>original: "Frucht"; the author uses the metaphor of a child or a ripened fruit to describe his intellectual work</note> to Your Most Glorious **Majesty** <note>original: "Majestät"</note>. After the borrowing comes nine, and after the birth, the distinction. The fruit is most diligently pleading that it did not come into being prematurely, and it intends for you to become its godfather. However, once it reaches its full age and size, it will be given even greater credit, so that it may always serve in the presence of your Crown and be eternal.

I also wish that it henceforth be held as the unique one ||: O Almighty King, who throughout the entire time of the birth was before my eyes with the most friendly **aspect** <note>original: "Aspectu," an astrological term referring to the favorable alignment of the stars or the King's own supportive gaze</note>, and thus this fruit, which I bring to light, I make very easy and humble :||; for that which I previously carried in my mind for seven whole years, I have now birthed quite fresh into this world and into the light in an interval of twelve days, by an incredible **magnetic power** <note>original: "virtute Magnetica," a Renaissance concept suggesting a spiritual or natural attraction that pulls ideas into physical form</note>.

May the Most Holy **Trinity** <note>original: "Dreifaltigkeit"</note> grant and permit that this may turn out fortunately and well for Your Great Might, and also for my **studies** <note>original: "Studijs"</note> of the pure and perfect truth; which Trinity etc., which from eternity with...

<summary>The author formally presents his work, titled "Solomon's Hieroglyphics," to his royal patron, describing it as a "fruit" that he carried mentally for seven years but finally "birthed" in just twelve days. He credits a "magnetic power" and a favorable "aspect" for the swift completion of the manuscript.</summary>

<keywords>Solomon's Hieroglyphics, dedication, gestation metaphor, magnetic power, Holy Trinity, alchemy of writing, royal patronage</keywords>

<meta>This page marks a significant transition in the manuscript. While the previous pages featured a 1667 devotional reflection by "Theophilus," this page begins a transcription (or translation into German) of John Dee’s famous 1564 work, "Monas Hieroglyphica." This suggests the manuscript owner was compiling a personal reference of esoteric and mystical knowledge.</meta>

# THE HIEROGLYPHIC
# MONAD
<note>original Latin: "Monas Hieroglyphica." The "Monad" refers to a single, indivisible entity that represents the unity of the universe.</note>

-> JOHN DEE OF LONDON. <-
<note>John Dee (1527–1608/09) was a famed mathematician, astronomer, and advisor to Queen Elizabeth I, deeply involved in occult philosophy.</note>

-> Explained mathematically, magically, cabalistically, <-
-> and anagogically <note>Anagogical interpretation seeks the hidden spiritual or mystical meaning of a text, typically relating to the soul's journey to the divine</note> to the <-

-> MOST WISE <-

# MAXIMILIAN, KING OF THE
# ROMANS, BOHEMIA, AND
# HUNGARY.
<note>Maximilian II (1527–1576), the Holy Roman Emperor, was known for his religious tolerance and interest in the sciences and arts.</note>

---

## THEOREM I.

All things which have either not yet existed or have lain hidden in the innermost parts of nature were first and most simply brought forth and represented with ease through a straight <term>line</term> <note>Latin: "Lineam"</note> <gloss>|</gloss> and through a <term>circle</term> <note>Early Modern German: "Circkel"</note>. <gloss>○</gloss>

<summary>This page serves as the title page and opening theorem of John Dee’s influential occult work, "Monas Hieroglyphica." It establishes the foundational idea that all of creation and nature's secrets can be expressed through the primary geometric forms of the line and the circle.</summary>
<keywords>John Dee, Monas Hieroglyphica, Maximilian II, Sacred Geometry, Hermeticism, Alchemy, Theorem I, Line and Circle</keywords>

--- Page 118 ---
<meta>This page continues the transcription of John Dee’s "Monas Hieroglyphica." While Theorem I introduced the basic geometric components of the universe (the line and circle), Theorems II and III begin to build those components into a cosmological system, identifying the point, the circle, and the center with the Earth and the Sun.</meta>

#### THEOREM II.

Yet, no <term>circle</term> <note>German: "Circulus"</note> can be artificially made without a straight <term>line</term> <note>Latin/German: "Lineam"</note>, and no straight line without a point. Therefore, both have begun their being and their essence in all things according to the nature of the point and of the **Monad** <note>The "Monad" here refers to the primary unit of existence, often represented by the number one or a single point.</note>. Those things which are surrounded by a circumference—of whatever kind they may be—cannot at all do without the service provided by the **Central Point** <note>Latin: "Punctum Centrale"</note>.

---

#### THEOREM III.

For this reason, the visible central point in our **Hieroglyphic Monad** shines within us and points to the Earth, around which both the Sun and Moon, along with the other planets, complete their orbits. 
<margin>Hieroglyphic Monad.</margin>
And since the Sun holds the highest rank <note>German: "Standt," referring to the Sun's dignity or status in the celestial hierarchy</note> in this, it is represented, because of its majesty <note>German: "Fürstlichkeit," literally "princeliness"</note>, by a perfect, complete circle with a visible **Center** <note>Latin: "Centro"</note>.

<image-desc>A hand-drawn diagram of the Monas Hieroglyphica symbol. It features a crescent (Moon) at the top, resting upon a circle with a central point (Sun). Below the circle is a cross (representing the four elements), which ends in two interconnected semi-circles resembling the horns of Aries.</image-desc>

<summary>These theorems explain the geometric necessity of the point and line in creating the Monad. The author establishes the Sun's symbolic importance—represented by a circle and center—within a geocentric cosmic model.</summary>
<keywords>Monas Hieroglyphica, Sacred Geometry, Central Point, Sun, Moon, Geocentrism, John Dee, Alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues John Dee’s exploration of the "Monas Hieroglyphica." Having established the geometric foundations of the point, line, and circle in Theorems I-III, Dee now moves to the relationship between the two primary celestial bodies in his symbol: the Moon (the crescent) and the Sun (the circle).</meta>

->MONAS HIEROGLYPHICA<-
<note>Hieroglyphic Monad</note>

## THEOREM IV.

Although here the Moon’s half-circle <note>The crescent shape at the top of the Monad symbol.</note> stands, as it were, higher and prior to the circle of the Sun, it nevertheless observes and respects the Sun as its Lord and King. It rejoices in assuming his form and proximity, striving to be his equal in the size of the **half-diameter** <note>Latin: *semidiametri*, or the radius of the circle.</note>. This is something the common <unclear>uninformed</unclear> person despises; yet the Moon always turns its face toward the Sun, desiring finally to be so mingled with the Sun’s rays that, through the light of the entire heavens, it is transformed into a kind of true Being. This continues until it appears once more in its given figure, as a certain **form** <note>Latin: *specie*.</note> following its celestial arc.

## THEOREM V.

We see, therefore, that in every night the Moon’s half-circle is <unclear>guarded</unclear>, so to speak, until it attains the perfection of the Sun. At that point, "out of evening and morning, a day was made." <note>A reference to the biblical Creation story in Genesis 1:5.</note> From this comes the first day, upon which was created the **Light of the Philosophers** <note>Latin: *LVX Philosophorum*. In the alchemical and hermetic tradition, this refers to the primordial light or the hidden spark of divinity and knowledge within matter.</note>.

## THEOREM VI.

We see here that the **Sun** <note>Latin: *Sol*.</note> and **Moon** <note>Latin: *Luna*.</note> stand upon a **cross** <note>German: *Creütz*. In Dee’s system, the cross represents the four elements—earth, air, fire, and water—and the intersection of the spiritual and material worlds.</note>.

<summary>Theorems IV through VI describe the mystical and geometric relationship between the Sun and Moon within the Monad symbol, emphasizing the Moon's desire to unite with the Sun's perfection to produce the "Light of the Philosophers."</summary>

<keywords>Moon, Sun, half-diameter, Light of the Philosophers, alchemy, geometry, celestial hierarchy, Genesis, the Cross, elements</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
<meta>This page continues John Dee’s exploration of the geometric and mystical components of the Monad symbol. While the previous page focused on the Sun and Moon (the circle and crescent), Theorem VI introduces the Cross at the base of the symbol, analyzing it through the lens of sacred numbers—specifically the three, the four, and the seven.</meta>

# THEOREM VI.

Concerning straight lines which, for our Hieroglyphic purpose, can be considered as both the **Ternary** <note>The number three or a group of three.</note> and the **Quaternary** <note>The number four or a group of four.</note>. Namely, the **Ternary**, which consists of <gloss>2</gloss> two straight lines and <gloss>1</gloss> one point common to them both, acting as their union. The **Quaternary**, however, is composed of <gloss>4</gloss> four straight lines enclosing four right angles, such that from each angle it is repeated <unclear>twice</unclear>: the location is thus entirely hidden, as is the **Octonary** <note>The number eight. Dee is likely referring to the eight directions or lines created if one considers the lines of the cross extending in both directions from the center.</note>, at least I doubt whether it was ever noticed or seen by our ancestors, the **Magi** <note>original: "Magis". Wise men or practitioners of high ancient learning.</note>, although it should be particularly noted.

Among the <gloss>1</gloss> first <gloss>2</gloss> fathers and <unclear>sages</unclear>, the <gloss>3</gloss> **Magical TERNARY** <note>original: "TERNARIVS Magicus".</note> contained within itself a <gloss>4</gloss> **BODY**, <gloss>5</gloss> **SOUL**, and <gloss>6</gloss> **SPIRIT** <note>original: "CORPVS", "ANIMAM", and "SPIRITVM". In alchemy, these three components must be united to achieve perfection.</note>. Therefore, we clearly have the first noble <gloss>7</gloss> **SEPTENARY** <note>original: "SEPTENARIVM". The number seven.</note>, namely from two straight lines and one common point <note>2 lines + 1 point = 3 (Ternary).</note>, and thereafter from four straight lines that branch out from a single point <note>The four arms of the cross.</note>; <unclear>from that, six and one makes seven</unclear> <note>Dee is calculating the "seven" by adding the three components of the Ternary to the four lines of the Quaternary.</note>.

---

# THEOREM VII.

When the **Elements** <note>original: "Elementa". The four classical elements: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</note> are displaced from their natural positions, then their parts of a similar nature, which have been separated from one another, learn to experience again what the **Elements** so naturally... <note>The text here refers to the Aristotelian idea that elements seek their "natural place" (e.g., earth falls, fire rises) when disturbed.</note>

<summary>Dee explains how the cross at the base of the Monad represents the sacred numbers three, four, and seven, linking geometry to the alchemical trinity of Body, Soul, and Spirit. He also begins to discuss the natural movement and restoration of the four Elements.</summary>
<keywords>Ternary, Quaternary, Septenary, Octonary, Magi, Body Soul and Spirit, Cross, Elements, sacred geometry, Monas Hieroglyphica</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>This page continues the geometric analysis of the Monad symbol's base. Whereas the previous page introduced the cross as a combination of two lines and a point (the Ternary) or four lines meeting (the Quaternary), this page deepens the connection between these lines, the four elements, and the mystical mathematics of the number ten.</meta>

## THEOREM VII.

After I have previously demonstrated through straight lines, it will not be unsuitable [to explain] through these straight lines, which proceed from a single and indivisible point into the opposing directions of the four elements: into which all things that come from those four elements can finally be resolved. Shaped and Ever-living—you should also diligently note this—that the points teach how a line arises from the point’s flow or flux <note>The idea that a point moving through space creates a line.</note>, as I have now shown here in the same manner. Since our elemental lines must measure that which is understood as a **physical point** <note>original: "punctum physicum"</note>, and whose occurrence is like a flow, produced as witnesses in our art of **Mechanical Magic** <note>original: "Mechanischen Magia"</note>.

## THEOREM VIII.

The Cabalistic expansion of the **QUATERNARY** <note>original: "QUATERNARII". A set of four.</note> according to the common way of speaking: when we say one, two, three, four, it gives in a sum the **DENARY** <note>original: "DENARIVM". The number ten.</note>, just as **Pythagoras** was also accustomed to say (1, 2, 3, 4 added together make ten). For this reason, it is not in vain that the cross made of straight lines—which is the twenty-first letter in the Latin alphabet <note>The letter 'X', which represents the number ten in Roman numerals and is the 21st letter of the alphabet in Dee's time.</note>—is formed from four straight lines. This was accepted by the ancient **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophis"</note>, that the number ten dwells within it; whether I have also assigned it here for that reason...

<summary>Dee explains how the lines of the cross represent the four elements emerging from a single point and demonstrates the mystical 'Tetractys' where the numbers 1, 2, 3, and 4 sum to 10 (the Denary).</summary>
<keywords>Quaternary, Denary, Pythagoras, elements, point and line, Mechanical Magic, Roman numeral X, Cabala</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the geometric and alchemical analysis of the Monad. Having established the "Denary" (the number ten) as being hidden within the cross in the previous page, the author now moves to discuss the Ternary (three) and Septenary (seven), and how the astrological sign of Aries (the ram) represents the necessary application of fire in the work.</meta>

# The **TERNARY** <note>original: "Ternarius"; a group of three</note>, which conducts and establishes its power through the **SEPTENARY** <note>original: "Septenarium"; a group of seven</note>,

## THEOREM IX.

It is observed, however, that this accords very well with the Sun and Moon of our **Monad**. From these—through the **Magic of the four Elements** <note>original: "Elementorum Magiam"</note>—a proper separation or division into two **lines** <note>original: "Lineas"</note> is undertaken. Thereafter, through those same lines, a further circular **Conjunction** <note>The astronomical or alchemical joining of two bodies</note> is granted in the perfection of the Sun. For one may describe an angle according to the size of any given Sun; then one can see the arc, and how much the **Denary proportion** <note>original: "Denaria proportio"; referring to the mystical number ten found in the cross</note> of the cross of the Sun and Moon is useful to our **Monad**.

## THEOREM X.

This **figure of Aries** <note>original: "Arietis figura"</note>, the zodiacal sign, which is shaped like a sharp point or spike and used by **Astronomers**, is known to all. It is well known that from this place in the heavens, the beginning of the **fiery triplicity** <note>In astrology, the three signs associated with fire: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius.</note> is noted. Therefore, in order to signify that in this **practice of the Monad** <note>original: "Monadis praxi"</note> the aid of fire is required, we have added and placed the **Astronomical** sign for Aries alongside it. Thus, we have briefly set down one part of the **Hieroglyphic explanation** <note>original: "Hieroglyphischen Erklärung"</note> of our **Monad**.

<summary>The author explains how the Monad symbol incorporates the Sun, Moon, and the sign of Aries, signifying that the "Magic of the Elements" and the application of fire are essential to the alchemical process.</summary>

<keywords>Ternary, Septenary, Monad, Elements, Conjunction, Denary, Aries, fire, triplicity, Hieroglyphics</keywords>

# Moon

<note>original: "Luna"</note>

In the hieroglyphic <term>Hieroglyphic: The author uses this to mean a symbolic, mystical language where shapes reveal the hidden nature of reality.</term> manner, it will not be difficult at all to see where this foundation of ours is specifically placed.

| ♃ | Jupiter <note>original: "Iupiter"</note> | ♀ | Venus |

We wish first to treat clearly those signs which contain the **characters of the Moon** <note>original: "Characteres des Monden"; referring to the crescent shape found within other planetary symbols</note>; afterwards, we will also speak of the characters of the Sun.

Our **Lunar nature** <note>The "Lunar" principle in alchemy usually represents the feminine, receptive, or silver-related aspect of matter.</note> (which has never been made subject to us because of the opposition of the elements) is mystically called **Saturn** <note>original: "Saturnus"</note>; and for that very same reason, she also bore the name of **Jupiter** <note>original: "Jovis"</note>. This same hidden figure remained our **Moon** <note>original: "Lunam"</note> daily; then monthly, when she was "elemented" <note>brought into a relationship with the four physical elements</note> for the third time, they noted her in the same way they are accustomed to calling **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note>. 

How profoundly high this is before our eyes! Although some wise men would have this represented through the **fourth revolution** <note>likely referring to a fourth stage of alchemical rotation or a celestial cycle</note>, this shall not be contrary to our most secret intention. We see that the purest **Magical Spirit** <note>original: "Magicus Spiritus"</note> in fact governs the pure work in the Moon. This spirit speaks its spiritual power with us alone, as if through the middle of a natural day, without words in a hieroglyphic manner, so that the conscious part is designated among these four <unclear>Geogamic</unclear> <note>This likely refers to "Geogamic" points, a term relating to the "marriage" or union of Earthly and celestial forces.</note> points.

## THEOREM XIII.

For is it not true that the **secret character of Mars** <note>original: "Geheimbte Character Martis"</note> is composed from the **hieroglyph of the Sun** <note>original: "Hieroglyphico der Sonnen"</note> with the assistance [of the Moon], so that a portion of the **elemental cross** <note>The cross (+) representing the four elements: earth, air, fire, and water.</note> falls to its future? And has not the **character of Venus** <note>original: "Veneris Character"</note> also come from the complete composition of the **Sun** <note>original: "Solis"</note> and the elements? Therefore, these turnings of the Sun and the Moon follow their fiery, life-honoring work, which perhaps through the progression of the other **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> allows it to be seen that each is [derived] from our bodily cross; namely, when the **light**

### THEOREM XIV.

It is clearly proven that the entire **Great Work** <note>original: "Magisterium"; the ultimate goal of the alchemical process</note> rests upon the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>original: "Sole und Luna"</note>. **Hermes the Thrice-Great** <note>original: "Termaximus Hermes," or Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note> also reminded us of this when he said that his father is the Sun and the Moon is his mother. However, we know from the present [work] that in the **Lemnian Earth** <note>original: "Terra Lemnia"; a reddish medicinal clay from the island of Lemnos, used here as a symbol for the "prime matter" or the physical foundation of the work</note>, there is a joining of the **Monad** with the rays of the sun, just as a special **influence** flows into that same Earth.

### THEOREM XV

Therefore, we present to the Philosophers, for the Canon and the common work, that which they have submitted to us from other parts of the month where, while the Sun travels in **Aries** <note>original: "Widder"</note>, then within us, the sign of the **Bull** <note>original: "Stier"; Taurus</note> assumes a new glory of light and becomes **exalted** <note>A term in astrology indicating a sign where a planet's influence is most harmonious and powerful</note> above its innate power. This power, which the Creator established by nature in ancient times, is more celestial; the ancients have given the Bull its name and its celestial **character** <note>The astrological symbol for Taurus</note>. The manner in which this sign is the exaltation of the **Moon** <note>original: "Luna"</note> is well known and manifest, as it has been incorporated into the books of astronomers since the earliest times of mankind. But this secret is understood only by those who have become skillful masters in this mystery, just as they have likewise said that **Taurus** is the house of **Venus**—namely, of joyful, fertile, marital love. For already the nature of the <unclear>Kalm</unclear> <note>Likely referring to a specific alchemical state or substance, perhaps related to "calm" or a cooling process</note>, as the great **Ostanes** <note>A legendary ancient Persian magus and alchemist often cited in Hermetic literature</note> describes in his hidden secrets, [is revealed]. But concerning the **Sun**, [we see] the manner in which it—the Sun—after certain burdens permitted to its spirit, takes on a noticeable strength and intends to triumph in that same house—namely, in our Bull—just as if it were in its own exaltation.

### ANNOTATION

I deem two things necessary to note here. The first concerns the piece, the **Hieroglyphic Figure**, and the true meaning of the Greek **diphthong** <note>A sound formed by the combination of two vowels in one syllable</note> **'ai'** <note>original: "αι"</note>, which in the first declension is always long in the **genitive** <note>original: "G.to"; a grammatical case indicating possession or source</note> singular. Secondly, if it is correctly understood, it points us toward the **alpha** <note>The first letter of the Greek alphabet, often symbolizing beginnings or the Divine</note> in two ways: either that the half-circle touches two circles alone, or, as it is drawn here, they intersect and cut through one another.

# THEOREM XVI.

It is useful that we now **philosophize** a little regarding the **Cross** <note>original: "Creytz"; here referring to the cross-shaped component at the base of the Hieroglyphic Monad</note> in a way that serves our purpose, even though our Cross is not [simply] composed of two straight lines that cross one another transversely. Nevertheless, lines placed together in such a way reach an equal length. We have intended to have both equal and multiple parts in the elegant **division** <note>original: "Ausstheilung"</note> of our Cross. We demonstrate through this how the power of the Cross possesses such equal lines, [and how it] is hidden in the strength of these two which are inscribed in this manner, because they have the same magnitude. For when it is said in the most general sense that one should make a cross from two equal straight lines, the justice of nature almost requires that the cross be made with an equal **partition** <note>original: "Zertheilung"</note> of the lines. 

According to this rule of justice, we wish to show the following figures for consideration, regarding this equal-lined Cross which is the **XXI.** <note>21st</note> letter in the Latin **Alphabet** <note>The letter 'X'. In the classical Latin alphabet of Dee's time, 'X' was the 21st letter (A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, I, K, L, M, N, O, P, Q, R, S, T, V, X)</note>. If one considers a straight line passing through the common point of the partition (and through the opposing **angles** <note>original: "Winckel"</note>, wherever they may be) in our Cross—which is made of straight lines, right angles, and equal sides—then the parts of the Cross made in this way will be completely and entirely equal on both sides of the straight line passing through it; thus:

From this figure, which emerges in a twofold way when a cross is correctly grasped and divided in such a manner:

Everyone certainly knows that twenty times five results in [one hundred], just as the letter itself is the **twentieth** in our alphabet and the **fifth** among the vowels.

But now we wish to consider the **Cross** with equal lines in another form; namely, as it stands in our **Monadic crowns** <note>Referring to the crescent or "horns" at the top of the Monad symbol, or perhaps the base</note>.
