# THEOREM XVI

The <gloss>2</gloss> L <gloss>1</gloss> <note>Referring to the shape of the letter L as a right angle</note> and its inversion, which according to old Latin custom is used for **fifty**, shows itself immediately before our eyes; however, since this same letter is also the mark of the **QUINARY** <term>quinary: the number five</term>, it is unfailingly taken from our **Denarius** <term>denarius: the number ten, represented by the Cross or X</term> of the Cross. But the Cross is situated in such a way that it is marked most perfectly and most secretly within the **Hieroglyphics** <note>Referring to the *Monas Hieroglyphica* symbol</note>; and one thought therefore that because this letter of the **Denarius** contains the power of the **Quinary** within itself, so God will simultaneously [be] to the **Quinquagenary** <term>quinquagenary: the number fifty</term> as his birth or house. O my God, how great is this mystery, and how His name signifies **EL** <note>original: "EL"; the Hebrew name for God, often associated with the number 50 in certain cabalistic traditions</note>. Indeed, I maintain that the Cross possesses the tenth power of the Cross because it stands at the midpoint between the first letters of the alphabet and the **Denarius** of the Cross, and is one that passes from one into the other in the order of tens. And this will show that in the Cross there are two such whole or perfect fives: so that if we consider only their numerical power, it is clear as day that the **Centenary** <term>centenary: the number one hundred</term> grows out of them. If they are multiplied into one another according to the **Rule of Squares** <note>original: "Quadrat Regel"</note>, they will give us **two thousand five hundred**, which **Square** <note>original: "Quadratus"; refers to 50 squared</note> is set and applied for itself from the previous **circular square number** <note>Referring to the mathematical properties of 5 (the quinary), which "circles" back to itself when squared (5x5=25)</note>; this will also bring forth the **Centenary**, so that the Cross may also be recognized as a **Centurio** <term>centurio: a centurion or commander of one hundred</term>, because it...

# THEOREM XVI

[The Cross] explains itself according to the power of its **Denarius** <term>denarius: the number ten, represented by the Roman numeral X</term>, and since there is but a single character or letter of the Cross here, it also signifies only one. Of these things we have been taught here, concerning this theory of the Cross: to count and progress over other things, even celestial ones, in this manner: One, Ten, Hundred, Thousand. For the denarian proportion of the Cross leads us upward in this way.

---

-> ### THEOREM XVII <-

Just as it can be evident from the sixth theorem that four right angles are to be considered in our Cross, and that each of these has the meaning of the **Quinary** <term>quinary: the number five, often represented as a right angle 'L' in this system</term>, as the immediately preceding theorem teaches us; so, when they are each placed in one way, but have a different position <gloss>location</gloss>, that same theorem allows that they may also become the **Hieroglyphic Sign** <note>original: "Hieroglyphica Nota"</note> of the fifteenth number. Thus it is most clear that the Cross commonly signifies ten; thereafter, that in the order of the Hebrew alphabet it is the twenty-first letter. From this it follows that the **Wise Men** <note>original: "Sophi"</note>, who are also called **Kabbalists** <term>Mecubales: from the Hebrew 'Mekubbalim', those who have received traditional mystical secrets</term>, have signified twenty-one by that same letter. Since the Cross can also be understood and considered in the simplest way—as a sign, as a single letter—whether it possesses an essential power or some other great force, from all of this we see that even the most profound **Cabalistic** connection can occur.

# THEOREM XVIII.

From the XII and XIII of our **Theorems** <note>original: "Theorematum"</note>, one can understand that **Celestial Astronomy** <term>Astronomia: here referring not just to the study of stars, but to the divine laws governing the universe</term> is like a mother and teacher to the inner man. When we now look secretly into the heavens with **Kabbalistic eyes** <term>Cabalistic eyes: a method of interpretation that seeks hidden spiritual truths behind physical appearances</term>, which we have obtained through the essence of the preceding mysteries, we shall truly see such an **Anatomy of our Monad** <note>John Dee refers to the "anatomy" of his symbol—the Monas Hieroglyphica—to show how it contains the components of the entire universe</note>. This anatomy constantly reveals itself in the Light and Life of Nature, and of its own accord, it openly brings forth the most hidden **mysteries** <note>original: "Misteria"</note> in their natural resolution.

We have been taught that it is for the better to belong to such a coordination, as we have observed the **theories** <note>original: "Theoricos"</note> and celestial motions of the Heavenly Sphere. How it leads its course in the heavens in the **shape of an egg** <note>original: "Eies gestalt"; this refers to the *Ovum Philosophorum* or Philosopher's Egg, a symbol of the cosmos and the alchemical vessel of transformation</note> is well known to the **Astronomers**. And because what has been said should suffice for a wise man, here is the interpretation of our **Triad of Celestial Characters** <term>Trias: a group of three, representing the three distinct sections of Dee's symbol (the lunar crescent, the solar circle, and the elemental cross)</term>, which we also present **hieroglyphically** <note>original: "Hieroglyphisch"; Dee used this term to describe his symbol as a "sacred carving" containing compressed knowledge</note>, and which perfectly agree with what has come before. From this reminder, the **Arithmeticians** <term>Arithmetici: those who study the mystical and mathematical properties of numbers</term> shall—

# BOOK XVIII.

Alchemists can recognize their various **antics** <note>original: "Bossen"; refers to tricks, buffoonery, or deceptive practices in the trade</note>, distinguishing every part of the spice for what it is—what the **oil of camelina** <term>Bördotter: also known as "Gold-of-pleasure" oil, derived from the Camelina sativa plant and used in early modern pharmacy and alchemy</term> is, or what the **lime of heads** <note>original: "Kalck von Köpfen"; likely referring to the calcined remains of skulls or the "caput mortuum" (dead head) left in a flask after distillation</note> consists of. Even the most inexperienced frauds should proceed with their disdain, and much more of the like; for this is the powder that the **Scraper** <note>original: "Schrober"; a symbolic figure, possibly a scraper or cleaner, contrasted here with the "Schroter" or Beetle</note> dared to cast out. This was done on account of the injustice that the **Fox**—having taken thought of this bird—inflicted with great strangeness through the fault of ignorant and guilty men.

Although some have stood by to add help to the **Beetle’s** <term>Schroter: specifically the Stag Beetle (Lucanus cervus). In alchemical emblems, the beetle often represents the sun or the process of putrefaction and rebirth</term> lot, they were not released from their burden because of it. But the **Scraper** alone believed that he should avenge this injustice in every way. Therefore, just as he had prepared a corrupt mind with much malicious intent, he did not lack the **kindling** <note>original: "Zündtfedern"; literally "igniting feathers" or triggers/fuses</note> or the power of understanding.

Thus, the Scraper pursued the **Bittern** <note>original: "Bitter"; refers to the Bittern bird (Rohrdommel), often used in alchemical allegory for its booming cry and its association with marshes/water</note> in a most merciless manner, using much insult and ill-treatment, finally bringing things to such a pass that the Bittern's house—even though it had been placed in **Jupiter’s lap** <note>original: "Jovis Schoß"; a reference to the classical fable of the Beetle and the Eagle, where the beetle destroys the eagle's eggs even when they are placed in Jupiter's protection</note>—was set on fire at all four corners and destroyed. In this and other ways, the Scraper finally slaughtered the whole essence which exists upon the earth—had **Jupiter** not prevented such evil and ordained that, at the time of year when the Bitterns diligently tend their **eggs**, then...

It warns against the deceptions of "frauds" who do not understand the true nature of their materials.</summary>

<header># CHAPTER VII</header>
<warning>Faded ink and significant bleed-through from the reverse side of the leaf.</warning>

The **Separator** <note>original: "Scheider"; a technician who separates metals, but here likely refers to a specific allegorical figure or a stage of "parting" in the work</note> might think to shorten things here, yet because I [desire] clarity, having been graciously struck by this bird, so that they have it from the **Sun-beetle** <term>Heliocantharis: from the Greek 'helios' (sun) and 'kantharos' (beetle); the alchemical scarab, symbolic of the sun’s path and the process of self-generation</term> in such a way that they always carry the youth of time hidden from me; this is to prove their very least art, to those who would like nothing better than if they themselves did not do it now. For with their upbringing, with signs that might be seen on them from behind, and that I do not say as a **King** what I possess, that I act as a **Philosopher**, but much more as an **Oedipus** <note>Oedipus: the mythological solver of the Sphinx's riddle; in alchemy, the "Oedipus" is the one who successfully deciphers the encrypted secrets of the Great Work</note> among those who were known or present, in whose sense and mind such a future comes, that they have thus fabled of the highest secrets of **Nature**.

I know that everyone finds the help that follows the scribe's art. When they now [possess] such a dissolved **Zinc** and **Juices**, [and] the same fortune with the **pure white** <note>original: "Lautern weißen"; likely referring to the albumen of the "Celestial Egg" or the purified white stage (Albedo) of the matter</note> and whole temperament for eating; thereafter the same temperament with the **Bittern’s** <note>original: "Bitters"; refers to the bird mentioned on the previous page, whose "spirit" is used here as a catalyst or solvent</note> whole spirit, shown according to artistic order, so that they translate it here and there, just as the little beetles make their **pills** <term>pilluleñ: small pellets or balls; a reference to the dung-ball rolled by the scarab, which alchemists compared to the formation of the Stone or the movement of the celestial spheres</term>. Thus would a great transformation of the day be presented, truly so that the white of the egg would no longer be seen, and would be immediately transformed into the **juice of the yolk**. Now, it may be that such **seeds** can always be caught therewith, which **Nature** will distribute orderly, without...

# THEOREM XVIII.

A certain **Hieroglyphic Sign** <note>original: "Hieroglyphicum Signum"; referring to the Monad, a composite symbol representing the unity of the cosmos</note> of this art should not be unknown here, of which neither Jews nor Heathens—nor even much more excellent and wiser philosophers—were even named as knowing before this time. Thus, it is very certain and true that **Anaxagoras** <note>A pre-Socratic Greek philosopher who proposed that all things are composed of infinitely divisible particles; here, he is claimed as an alchemical initiate</note> indeed attained enough from this **Mastery** <term>Magisterio: the "Great Work" or the perfected process of alchemy</term> for **Medicine** and for every practical conclusion, as is attributed to him in his book on *Natural Changes*. And whoever would truly apply their understanding to this would clearly see that all such things are based upon the power of our **Hieroglyphic Monad** <note>original: "Monadis Hieroglyphicae"; the specific symbol designed by John Dee to represent the Sun, Moon, Elements, and Fire</note>.

---

# THEOREM XIX.

That the **Sun** and the **Moon** pour their bodily powers into all lower **elemental bodies** <note>original: "Elementische Corpora"; the physical objects of the earthly realm composed of the four elements</note> much more strongly than all other planets is proven in the Work and in the power of the dissolution of all physical things. This occurs according to the order of the **regimen of fire** <note>original: "Feuer Zeit"; the specific timing and intensity of heat applied during the alchemical process</note> when they pour down watery moisture from the Moon and the fiery juice of the Sun, through both of which the victory of mortals and dense turbidity are maintained.

<vocab>Hieroglyphicum, Anaxagoras, Magisterio, Medicinam, Monadis Hieroglyphicae, Sonn, Mond, Planeten, Elementische Corpora</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces Theorem XVIII and XIX, identifying the 'Hieroglyphic Monad' as the central key to the work and citing Anaxagoras as a historical precedent. It explains the dominant influence of the Sun and Moon's 'juices' in the dissolution and transformation of elemental matter.</summary>
<keywords>Hieroglyphic Monad, John Dee, Anaxagoras, Alchemical Mastery, Sun and Moon, Elemental Bodies, Regimen of Fire, Dissolution</keywords>

# THEOREM XX.

Since, however, in the previously established and proper hieroglyphic manner, it has been demonstrated that the **Elements** <term>Elementa: the four fundamental components of nature—Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</term> are signified by whole lines, we wish here to provide a proper and precise **speculation** <note>original: "Speculation"; here meaning a deep philosophical or theoretical investigation</note> or consideration of our **point of points** <note>original: "Punctu puncti"; the central, indivisible dot from which all geometry begins</note>. This point is situated exactly at the center. In our consideration of the **Ternary** <term>Ternary: the number three, representing the spiritual triad or a state of balance</term>, the point cannot be omitted at all. This is especially true when our **Binarius** <term>Binarius: the number two, representing duality or the division of unity into opposites</term> stands in such a way that none of this divine number is lacking—which <gloss>1</gloss> is understood <gloss>2</gloss> therein <gloss>2</gloss> as being defective <gloss>2</gloss> by saying it can be omitted.

We wish to state it thus: as if the point were also there, for then our **Binarius** will no longer be applicable here, but rather a **Quaternarius** <term>Quaternarius: the number four, representing the material world and the four elements</term> will emerge. This is because by the removal of that point, the unity of the lines is divided. 

But our adversaries have said that the **Binarius** could remain. Against this, the **Binarius** and **Quaternarius** struggle to be one and the same in a single consideration; however, <gloss>2</gloss> it is <gloss>1</gloss> sufficiently obvious that such a thing is impossible. Therefore, the **juncture** <note>original: "junctum"; the intersection or "joint" where the lines of a cross meet</note> must <gloss>2</gloss> necessarily <gloss>1</gloss> be present, which <gloss>1</gloss> with <gloss>2</gloss> the **Binarius** makes our **Ternary**, and represents that same thing. Nothing else can be put in its place. Yet, it is not part of the essential property of the **Binarius**, nor is it in any way a "piece" of it. And that it is no piece of the **Binarius** is thus proven: all pieces of a line are lines, but this is a **juncture**...

<note>The argument here is a geometric one: if you have two lines forming a cross (the Binarius), the point where they meet is a unique entity. If you count the lines and the point, you move from 2 to 3 (Ternary). If you consider the four arms created by that center point, you arrive at 4 (Quaternarius).</note>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "double Moon" or Aries symbol at the top of the Monad. It shifts from the external shape to the internal "degrees" and the relationship between the lunar crescents and the solar circle, emphasizing how the "Art of the Elements" and the concept of movement are required to achieve alchemical perfection.</meta>

When we now notice here that the innermost **degrees** <note>original: *gradus*; refers to stages of intensity or refinement in alchemy</note> are in the **Moon** <note>original: *Luna*</note>, we shall say: The Moon is the spirit, the body is the living Moon. It seems, however, that life is more closely accompanied by or endowed with **movement** <term>movement</term> <note>original: *Motu*</note>; that is, just as each life is a movement. There appear to be six kinds of movement <note>Likely a reference to the six Aristotelian types of motion: generation, destruction, increase, decrease, alteration, and change of place</note>, all of which are well known. 

The **Cross** <note>original: *Creuz*</note> which is placed here, hidden behind, indicates that the **Art of the Elements** <note>The practice of manipulating the four elements—fire, air, water, and earth—to achieve transformation</note> is required here; but this we have learned in our theory: just as the half-circle of the Moon is **hieroglyphic** <note>original: *Hieroglyphica*</note>, so also the whole circle signifies the **Sun** <note>original: *Sonn*</note>. 

Here, however, there are two half-circles, yet they are divided; nevertheless, they stand joined together at a common point. If these two half-circles are joined together fittingly—as we are able to do through art—they can demonstrate to us and bring the formal perfection of a circle. When these things are considered together in this way, it is obvious that from this, we can hear this understanding or meaning expressed **hieroglyphically** <note>original: *Hieroglyphisch*</note> in summary: That the Moon, the living spirit, and the **Sun** <term>Sun</term> <note>original: *Sol*</note> must be handled through the mastery of the elements.

In the midst of it and around the seat were four beasts <note>original: "4. Thierer"</note>, each of which had six wings, and without rest day and night they sang and spoke:

> Holy, Holy, Holy is God the Lord Almighty, who was, and who is, and who is to come,

Before whom also the twenty-four elders <note>original: "24. Aeltester"</note> who sat upon twenty-four thrones fell down, casting off their golden crowns, worshipping Him and saying:

> Lord, You are worthy to receive glory and honor and power, for You have created all things, and by Your will they have their being and were created.

**Amen**, says the first letter of the <term>Delta <note>The Greek letter Δ. In this context, it likely refers to John Dee himself, who frequently used the triangle or "Delta" as a personal signifier, representing the number 4 and the Trinity.</note></term>: to whom God has given and bestowed the will and the skill to thus record this divine mystery in writing for eternal memory, and to bring such work of his to a happy conclusion

<summary>This page concludes the 24th Theorem by linking the mathematical 'Quaternary' to the 24 elders and 4 beasts of the Apocalypse, ending with a prayer of praise. The author uses the symbol of the Delta to signify the completion of the work through divine grace.</summary>

The first is the composition of the Point <note>original: *Puncti*</note>, yet it is

that which is indivisible and is contained within one—both one and
nothing else; it is no number <note>original: *Numerus*</note> and is yet a number,
and it is not counted and yet it is counted. Between it and one
is no number. In Unity <note>original: *Unitet*</note> it remains one, and through its
circumference it compels the Ternary <note>original: *Ternarium*; the number three, referring here to the three alchemical principles or the Trinity</note>, which, first encompassing it,
brings forth all things with a wondrous nature. Just
as it begins from a single seed, it cannot be
fully explained by any man, and by nothing but the truth
of God. It is the creature <note>original: *Creatur*</note> or the creation <note>original: *Geschöpff*</note>, the
very likeness <note>original: *Ebenbildt*</note> of the human image, neither living nor dead,
through which we are shown a wondrous
progression of all art and nature. And I say to you, O friend, in the
highest truth of God, that whoever is gifted with the simple
knowledge <note>original: *Erkäntnus*</note> of this pure simplicity, the same
shall be perfect in all natural hidden arts
and will find hidden, wondrous wisdom and
goodness—a progression toward which the desires of
invention and gratitude shall reach, so that this same help

<summary>Trithemius explains the mystical "Point" and "Unity" as the indivisible source of all creation, which gives rise to the alchemical "Ternary." He asserts that understanding this "pure simplicity" is the key to mastering all hidden natural arts and wisdom.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical and philosophical discourse by Johannes Trithemius, specifically expanding on how the "simple" Unity becomes a "composite" through addition. It maintains the paradoxical language of the previous page regarding the nature of Number and the "Point."</meta>

...through the same, not only do similar things occur, but also many dissimilar things. The composite is an element of nature, a simple "one-one," but through addition, the composite becomes manifold—that is, there are many types of "one" within it. Yet, it may nonetheless be brought back into water through fire, and from water into fire, and from fire back into a simple state <note>The author is describing the alchemical process of "reduction to the first matter," where complex substances are broken down into their primal, "simple" elements.</note>. 

And it is a Number <note>original: *Numerus*</note> and is counted, and yet it is no number and is not counted; for simple nature, through addition, becomes a composite thing or a Three <note>This refers to the "Ternary," the three alchemical principles of Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt emerging from the One.</note>, and by these it can be counted. For from the One, the sum is established, but it is counted from the unity of a unique and perfect enclosed [oneness] and is counted: one enclosed, one excluded, with a "threefold-one." This "one" is namely from the Souls of the World <note>original: *Seelen der Welt*; referring to the *Anima Mundi*, the world-soul that connects all living things.</note>, and the threefold Number <note>original: *dreyfaltige Numerus*</note> becomes—it seeks by nature to be at one with the end. 

A thing holds power within a powerful [force], and the other within a circle <note>original: *Zirkel*</note>; that is, it travels continually in a circle or globe, and yet remains fixed in a single Point <note>A reference to the "Center of the Circle," a common alchemical metaphor for God or the source of all matter.</note>. One does not imagine a Silver <note>In alchemy, Silver (Luna) represents the feminine, cooling, and reflective principle.</note> in this way <unclear>Cq. d.</unclear> unless it is first prepared. Reflect upon it thus: if it is purified by fire to its state of simplicity <note>meaning its pure, unmixed essence</note> and brought back through means or the appropriate preparatory tools, etc.

<meta>This page continues the technical and philosophical defense of the author’s methods, specifically focusing on the mathematical foundations mentioned at the end of the previous page. It is not merely a matter of curiosity how the causes of the effects of the most wonderful things occur—those which rest upon the laws of nature. This progression begins from Unity or the **Number of Unity** <note>original: *Unario Numero*; the number one, representing the source or the Monad in classical philosophy</note> through the **Binary** <note>original: *Binarium*; the number two</note> into the **Ternary** <note>original: *Ternarium*; the number three</note>, yet not before it rests within the **Quaternary** <note>original: *Quaternario*; the number four</note> through the order or governance of the degrees in union or simplicity. 

In this regard, the number 4 holds the secret known to the wise: that one must approach it only through its components, together with 1, 2, 3, and 4, which, when added together, make 10. This entire count is a perfect completion, for it proceeds as a transition back to the One. Indeed, beyond the **ten-part number** <note>This refers to the "Decad." In Pythagorean thought, the number 10 was the most sacred number, represented by the *Tetractys*, a triangle of ten dots</note>, there is no simpler number than 1, 2, 3, and 4 together with 10. Their further transition toward the unity of the simple laws does not advance those laws any further. 

Then, those who do not know what kind of beginning we use in the discovery of these wonderful things may marvel at the **celestial secrets** <term>original: *Sterngeheimnus*; literally "star-secrets," referring to the hidden influences of the heavens</term> of this composition. Such people believe that we attempt to bring these things about either with the help of **evil spirits** or through some other superstitious practice contrary to the Holy Christian faith. However, we have observed these things for no such reason, but rather for the sake of their pure truth, and we ourselves are not at all amazed by them <note>The author is asserting that these "miracles" are actually just natural science and math, so they do not seem supernatural to those who understand the underlying laws</note>,

84.

For as Holy Scripture indicates, from the inner knowledge of God—which no one receives except he who has received this Word and Sacrament through the Holy Spirit—there flows the outward and public confession and good works, which bear witness to such a confession of God in faith. In the same way, no one can learn these aforementioned things or make use of them, unless he has received a special <gloss>k</gloss> <note>The letter "k" here likely stands for *Kraft* (power/strength) or *Kenntnis* (knowledge)</note> through Divine Grace to understand these things. 

As it is with nature within nature and that which is in them: with the light of the fire, with the fire the radiance, with the radiance the power, with the power the wisdom or art <note>In this context, "art" refers to *alchemy* or the "sacred art" of natural philosophy</note>, and with the art the perfection of a healthy mind. Therefore, oh my friend, you who have also witnessed the circumstances of our life, you must perceive with your mind the temptation of ruses <note>original: *Rencklen*; referring to intrigues or deceptive tricks</note> and words; yet our vision and blamelessness shall remain free from the poisonous howling <note>original: *Geheüß*; likely a variant of *Geheul* (howling) or *Gehäuse* (casing/clamor), used here to describe the noise of critics</note> of the judges and priests <note>original: *Pfaffen*; a common, often pejorative term for clerics during this period</note>, and those who hate me, and I confess this. 

For I have, with Divine help, long since acted in the truth of such things, if it pleases you to join me in this. I do not desire this from you because I fear the changing whims of men, but rather because of the One before whose judgment we believe we will perhaps always stand. May He silence the words of my adversaries <term>original: *Widerpartz*; opponents or legal antagonists</term> through His unforgetfulness <note>original: *Unwiederdenkheit*; a term implying God’s constant memory and inescapable justice</note> toward the lying mouths they turn against us.

<meta>The author continues their self-defense, transitioning from the mathematical theories of the previous page to a personal declaration of their status as a monk and practitioner of "natural magic." ...might be horrified, then [by] all these things which I have never before in the days of my life let be heard; these same things I have done rightly with God and without any contradiction of the Christian faith. Although so few people are found who can understand the mystery of natural, hidden experience.

Who am I? I am a mortal man, that I know; a Christian in constant steadfastness of faith; a monk <term>Münch; a member of a monastic order</term> by my profession, having vowed in the monastery to leave nothing unused in good practice, diligently bearing the grace of helpful penance, etc. I am not useless, so that what is done to me occurs [rightly]; what does not fail, I believe and understand such that, comforted by things to come, I cannot think of death. Furthermore, as I mentioned above, there are few people with such experience who, throughout all lands, speak of the Art—some, for the sake of a few things, give my discovered art praise and honor, while others speak shame and vice of me.

But that I should not know the art of natural **magic** <note>original: *Magia*</note>, I cannot say; for I know it assists in all that is carried out, whatever wonders occur **naturally** <note>original: *Naturalis*</note> beyond natural knowledge through the realm of spirits. There is nothing in me but the Christian faith, which not nature, but the grace of God has granted me.

<meta>The author continues his self-defense by identifying with the famous scholar Albertus Magnus. He shifts from justifying his personal status as a monk to justifying his intellectual pursuits, specifically his study of "Magic" and "Natural Philosophy," which he views as a legitimate, though often misunderstood, path to wisdom.</meta>

85.

The most learned and, among the saints, truly a very holy man, Albertus—called by his surname "the Great" <term>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280); a Dominican friar and bishop who became one of the most influential medieval philosophers and scientists</term> because of his very deep understanding—who also, as one might worthily expect with good reason, through delving into the deep secrets of **Natural Wisdom** or **Philosophy**, experienced and recognized quite wonderful and unheard-of things beyond conventional teaching. This occurred to such an extent that he has been unfairly and wrongly held to be a sorcerer and a practitioner of the **black arts** <note>original: *Schwarz-künster*</note> by the inexperienced and laypeople, even to this very day.

**Magic** <note>original: *Magia*</note> or **Philosophy** was not unknown to him; he also understood many books of sorcery and superstition which are incomprehensible to common people and seem to lack a virtuous <note>The transcription notes 'tugentlichen' (virtuous or effective)</note> foundation. However, he himself was not evil or superstitious because of them. For wisdom and art <note>In this context, 'art' refers to practical skill or technical knowledge</note> are not evil in themselves, but the use of them is evil. Therefore, I will also suffer and endure with patience what such a man as he has endured, without sin.

I also do not deny that I know **secret things** <note>original: *Heimliche Dinge*; likely referring to the occult or hidden properties of nature</note>, although I am far below Albertus in erudition and learning, and in no way comparable to him in holiness. I must confess that I have read many books on the art of **magic** <note>original: *Magia*</note> and have investigated not a few experiments and practical techniques <note>original: *Handgriffe*; literally 'hand-grips' or manipulations</note>, including those very things which the learned...

<summary>The author defends his study of natural magic by citing Albertus Magnus as a precedent, arguing that the pursuit of hidden knowledge is not inherently sinful, though it is often misunderstood as sorcery by the unlearned.</summary>

One can neither grasp, distinguish, nor compare such writings. Consider also how many are confused by that which is known only of the natural art of **Magic**, men who are otherwise quite learned. --- Page 181 ---
<meta>The author continues his critique of those who practice alchemy and magic for greed, contrasting their failures with the true "Natural Magic" which is rooted in a specific, hidden unity. He begins to outline three "principles" or "entries" required for true understanding, focusing here on the first principle and its relationship to sacred numerology.</meta>

86.

...from this same conclusion, all their testing is in vain until they seek and find the **One** within the mystery <note>original: *Geheimnuß*</note> of wondrous Nature. We cannot, however, boast of confirmation through experiences <note>original: *Experientien*</note> as others do, who strut about claiming they might attain the art of **divination**. Yet these latter individuals will find themselves in a poor position before those who are truthful; those poor souls, however, since they follow the intention of their own purse to put the devil's art into practice, turn the proven and wisest medicines <note>original: *Artzen*; likely referring to medical treatises or remedies</note>—which were described by good, learned men in former times and which they cannot understand at all due to their own ignorance—into "false experiments."

**Mark this well:** In this natural philosophy <note>original: *Philosophia*</note> and **magic**, there are three secret and hidden **principles** <term>Einfänge; literally "entries" or "beginnings," referring here to foundational axioms or points of entry into the occult arts</term> without which no perfect knowledge of the workings can result in any fruitful progress.

The first **principle** lies in a single thing, which has no equal, but through which all power or virtue of all wondrous natural things is brought into an actual effect. Of this we have spoken; for the **One** coming from the **One** is neither composed nor changed. Toward it, from the **Septenary** <note>original: *Septenario*; the number seven</note> and the **Quaternary** <note>original: *Quaternario*; the number four</note>, a progression occurs toward the unhappy number <note>In Pythagorean and Hermetic thought, the number two (the dyad) was often considered "unhappy" or "unlucky" because it represented the first departure from Divine Unity into duality and division</note>. 89.

To the true Sun which is God, purified through the refinement <note>original: *leuterung*; refers to both spiritual purification and the alchemical process of refining base metals into gold</note> of faith, from the lowest [depths], may your mind be ignited in the longing of the soul with the fervor of the Almighty’s love; for the long rest of mortal love is but a short time, and it is better to love the Creator than to love the creature <note>original: *Geschöpff*; here meaning any created thing or person as opposed to the Divine Creator</note> in truth at all times. Given from Sponheim <note>A Benedictine monastery where Trithemius served as abbot</note> in my poor monastery, which you have seen, written with my poor hand at your first request, the tenth day of the month of May in the year of our Savior and Lord Jesus Christ, 1503.

# MONAS <note>The Monad; in mystical philosophy, this represents the "One," the source of all being or the Divine itself.</note>

wants to consider and show what the One and **Archetype** <note>the original pattern or model from which all things are made</note> be, and what nature and property **Unity** has. Secondly, after consideration of the first **Number**, now follows the property of the **BINARY** <note>the number two; representing the first step away from unity into multiplicity</note> which is **produced** out of **UNITY**, and thus a pillar is made, the **ANGELIC WORLD**, which is created from the first **Substanz**, immaterial. This has its own course—the immovable—yet strength and perfection, irreproachable <unclear>s:</unclear> good, full of bliss; and according to its domain, ordered into three **HIERARCHIES**. Just as such is then reported and shown by the names of Perfection, Illumination, and Purity. <meta>The manuscript transitions here from the linguistic and philosophical discussion of a universal alphabet seen on the previous page to a technical treatise on alchemy. # Theory of the Materials of the Philosophers' Stone.

The Sulphur of Gold <note>original symbol: $\odot$; in alchemy, this symbol represents both the Sun and Gold. Here, "Sulphur" refers not to common brimstone, but to the active, "soul" principle of the metal.</note> is, according to the indications of the experienced philosophers, a mineral—yet it is not a metal—from which mineral the Stone of the Philosophers is prepared. This Stone contains within itself Gold [$\odot$], in which is held the spirit and the "first matter" <note>The *materia prima*, or the primitive, unformed base of all substance.</note> of Gold, which is adorned with the ornaments of Gold [$\odot$] and can be converted into Gold [$\odot$]. For Gold [$\odot$] is the noblest among all metals and is shaped toward the white and toward the red <note>Refers to the *albedo* (whiteness) and *rubedo* (redness), the final two stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note>, in which is the most noble Sulphur. Therefore, one should select the pure [Gold], also using Antimony <note>original: "Antimonij"; Antimony was frequently used in the "stibium" process to purify gold by consuming other base metals.</note>, so that no other metal may be found therein except Gold [$\odot$] alone. From this, the Sulphur is then drawn out of the spirit, which is a hidden and wonderful spirit without which our work cannot be accomplished. For this reason, one should not work in this Art except through the alone-purified Sulphur of Gold [$\odot$]. As the Masters in the *Assembly of the Philosophers* <note>original: "Turba"; refers to the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the earliest and most influential Latin alchemical texts, written as a transcript of a fictional assembly of pre-Socratic philosophers.</note> say: "You should know, meanwhile, that no body is clearer or purer than the Sulphur of Gold [$\odot$], for it has Gold [$\odot$] hidden within it." Just as a ruby has hidden within itself the coloration of all other precious stones, so also has the Sulphur of Gold [$\odot$] hidden within itself the virtue of all metals. It also contains the Gold [$\odot$] which is the head and a lord of all other metals; it is also the best and stands alone when others are compared to it in quality, etc.

<summary>This page explains the "Theory of Materials," specifically identifying the "Sulphur of Gold" as the essential starting point for creating the Philosophers' Stone. It describes gold as the noblest metal, containing a hidden spirit and the potential for both the "white" and "red" stages of alchemy.</summary>

<keywords>Sulphur of Gold, Philosophers' Stone, Materia Prima, Antimony, Turba Philosophorum, Albedo, Rubedo, Transmutation</keywords>

<meta>Continuing from the technical treatise on the "Sulphur of Gold" (page 95), this page details the specific laboratory operations required to achieve the "Great Work." This is the only place and way through which all transformation shall occur: through the destruction <note>In an alchemical context, "destruction" or "annihilation" refers to the breakdown of a substance's physical form to release its inner essence.</note> into its **first matter** <note>original: *materia prima*; the primitive, formless base of all substance.</note>. And thus, the gold is destroyed into its first matter, even more than nature [can do by] its own nature. Therefore, the glass [vessel] should be diligently sealed with its material and placed in a furnace with a very gentle fire. Then, the material at the top of the glass will become volatile like a smoke within 40 days; this is a sign that the **sulfur** has been changed by the **mercury**. 

Afterward, the fire should be strengthened a little, until the material is transformed into a coal or a stone, just like molten lead. In the transition between the white and black colors, many different colors will appear, caused by the vapors; however, the white eventually remains there above all others. Then, the fire should be strengthened again by one degree, until the white is changed into a red. Especially as the white [stage] passes, a yellow color will be seen before the red color appears. Increase the fire degree by degree until the red perfectly appears, with which it is crowned—a red as deep as a ruby. 

Then it has certainly and infallibly become a **tincture** <term>tincture: a potent alchemical substance, often a powder or oil, capable of transmuting base metals or healing the body</term>. And if this medicine is dissolved <note>original: *resolvirt*</note> and coagulated <note>original: *coagulirt*</note> again, and if this process of dissolving and coagulating is repeated often, the more its power to transmute <note>original: *tinquiret*; literally "to tint" or "to dye," referring to the changing of a metal's color and nature into gold.</note> increases. After the fourth solution and coagulation, it now transmutes 1,000,000 loth <term>loth: a traditional German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 15 grams or half an ounce</term>.

For if another "seed" were seen therein, it would be worthless; if it has no gold in it, it does not matter how much [work] you do.

# The Furnace of Hermes

<margin>
The furnace is called the **Athanor** <term>Athanor: from the Arabic *at-tannūr*, a "slow-burning" furnace designed to maintain a constant heat for weeks or months without constant attention</term> of the philosophers, and is also called the **Mountain of the Philosophers** <note>original: "mons philosophorum"</note>. 

Within it, the **Philosophers' Stone** <term>Lapis Philosophorum</term> is left to reside. It is constructed in this manner so that the fire remains inside—specifically for the sake of the Stone—for a certain time until it reaches the correct measure. This is done so that the **Spirit of the Gold** may leave the **Body** <note>In alchemy, the "Spirit" is the volatile essence, while the "Body" is the fixed, material remains. The goal is often to separate, purify, and then reunite them.</note>. 

Then, it shall become a light upon the Body of the gold, carrying the spirit into heaven. This is the fire that burns upon the **Mountain of Handiwork** <note>original: "Berg der handthun"; this likely refers to the "Mountain of the Great Work," a metaphorical site of alchemical transformation</note>. For many, this is known upon this very ground.

Greetings from the House of Laben <note>original: "Gruß Cassa de Laben"</note>
The year 1574, 
on the 12th of March.
</margin>

</insert>

The **Vessel of Hermes** <note>original: "faß Hermetis"; refers to the sealed glass vessel or "Philosophical Egg" where the transformation occurs</note> in which the Spirit is held along with the material.
</insert>

<insert>
In the tripod; this is the **ash-bed** <term>ash-bed: a layer of sifted ashes used to distribute heat evenly around a glass vessel to prevent it from cracking</term> in which the vessel stands.
</insert>

<insert>
Put the soot there, through which the **youth** <note>original: "die jung"; likely a reference to the *filius philosophorum* or "Philosophical Child," a symbolic name for the Stone as it begins to form</note> thereafter emerges.
</insert>

<insert>
Close the little doors and the **air-draft** <note>original: "Lust," likely a variant of "Luft" (air)</note>, where they "feed" through the fire.
</insert>

<insert>
Of the Furnace.
</insert>

<meta>This page transitions from the practical description of the Athanor furnace on page 54 to the foundational philosophical text of Western alchemy: the Emerald Tablet. Having explained how the physical fire must be managed, the author now provides the "secret words" that govern the spiritual and material transformation of the Great Work, attributed to the legendary figure Hermes Trismegistus.</meta>

# The Emerald Tablet of the Alchemy
## of Hermes Trismegistus

The secret words of **Hermes** <term>Hermes Trismegistus: a legendary Hellenistic figure combining the Greek god Hermes and the Egyptian god Thoth, credited with writing the foundational texts of alchemy</term> which were written upon an **Emerald Tablet** <note>original: "Smaragdinesche Taffeln"; according to legend, this brief text contained the entire secret of the universe and the Philosophers' Stone</note> which was found among his manuscripts in a dark hole <note>often interpreted as a "hidden vault" or "tomb"</note> in which his corpse was found buried. 

This is certain without falsehood and most true: that which is below is even as that which is above, and what is above is even as that which is below, for the accomplishment of the miraculous work <note>original: "Wunderwerck"</note> of the one thing. 

And just as all things come from one by the counsel and mediation of the one, so all things are born from this one thing through skill and preparation. Its father is the **Sun** <term>☉: the symbol for both the Sun and Gold; representing the active, masculine principle</term>, its mother is the **Moon** <term>☽: the symbol for both the Moon and Silver; representing the passive, feminine principle</term>. The wind and air carried it in its body; the **Earth** <note>original Latin: "Terra"</note> is its nurse. 

The father of all perfection <note>original: "Berathung"; in this context, it implies the "design" or "counsel" of the work</note> or the remedy of the whole world is this; its power is complete if it is turned toward the **earth** <note>original Latin: "terram"</note>. You shall separate the **earth** <note>original Latin: "terram"</note> from the **fire** <note>original Latin: "igne"</note>, the subtle from the gross, delicately and with great art and skill. 

Thus it rises from the earth toward heaven and descends again to the earth, receiving the power of both the superiors and the inferiors. In this way, you will possess the glory of the whole world; therefore all darkness shall flee from you. This is the strength of all strengths, for it will overcome every subtle thing and penetrate every hard and solid thing. 

Thus was the world created. From this shall come marvelous adaptations, of which this is the model.

<summary>This page presents the "Emerald Tablet," a foundational alchemical text outlining the principle of "As Above, So Below" and the symbolic birth of the Philosophers' Stone from the Sun and Moon.</summary>
<keywords>Emerald Tablet, Hermes Trismegistus, alchemy, Sun and Moon, Earth and Fire, macrocosm and microcosm, The Great Work</keywords>

# Here this has an end

->Therefore I have been called **Hermes Trismegistus**, because<-
->I possess the three parts of the philosophy of the whole world. Now<-
->that which I have said concerning the **Operation of the Sun** <note>original: "Operation von Solis"; in alchemy, the "Sun" (Sol) represents the ultimate goal: the creation of the Philosophers' Stone or the transmutation of base metals into gold</note> is fulfilled.<-

<note>The "three parts of philosophy" mentioned above traditionally refer to alchemy (the operation of the sun), astrology (the operation of the stars), and theurgy (the operation of the gods/divine magic), which together comprise the Hermetic wisdom of the universe.</note>

<summary>This page marks the conclusion of the Emerald Tablet, the foundational text of Western alchemy. It provides the traditional justification for the name "Thrice-Greatest" (Trismegistus) and confirms the completion of the spiritual and physical "Operation of the Sun."</summary>

# The Philosopher Hortulanus’ <note>Latin: "The Gardener." This is a pseudonym for a 14th-century alchemical commentator, traditionally identified as Joannes de Garlandia or Ortholanus.</note> Interpretation and Brief Explanation of the Maritime Gardens <note>original: "Hortis Maritimis"</note> regarding this Emerald Tablet of the Alchemy of Hermes Trismegistus.

## The Prayer of Hortulanus

> Glory, honor, and praise be to You, Lord Almighty God the Father, to Your beloved Son our Lord Jesus Christ, and to the Comforter, the Holy Spirit. You, Holy Trinity, faithful and one God—to You I, a mortal man, give thanks. Since I have recognized the transience of the house of this world, so that I would not be led astray by such pleasures, You have drawn me away from it through Your mercy. But because I see that many desire this art <note>The "art" refers to alchemy, viewed here as a sacred science of transformation.</note> and are worthy of it, so help me, Lord God, that I may lead and instruct my good and beloved friends on the right path using the understanding or wisdom which You have granted me. Thus, when they have witnessed the truth with their own eyes, they may praise and bless Your Holy and glorious name for all eternity, Amen.

### The Preface.

I, called Hortulanus, from the little gardens situated by the sea, who am entirely wrapped and clothed in the skin of Jacob <note>The "Skin of Jacob" is an alchemical metaphor. It can refer to the "Fleece" or the rough, outer appearance of the primary matter that hides the "gold" within, or the humble mantle of the philosopher.</note>...

# The Writings of Hermes the Thrice-Great

...not unfairly have I been called a disciple or student of the philosophers. I have been moved by the love of my good friend and have undertaken a general explanation of Hermes Trismegistus <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure, "Hermes the Thrice-Great," to whom the foundation of alchemical wisdom is attributed.</note>, who is the father of all philosophers. Whether it was before or after, and although it remains hidden, the King of the true operation <note>A reference to the successful completion of the alchemical process, or perhaps divine guidance in the work.</note> has happily revealed it to us through the movement of my fingers. For the concealment used by the philosophers in their writings helps not at all, where the teaching of the Holy Spirit is effective and holy.

---

## That the Art of Alchemy is True
-> and certain, Chapter 1. <-

The Philosopher says *Verum* <note>original: "Verum" - Latin for "True." This refers to the first word of the Emerald Tablet.</note>: it is true that the art of alchemy is given without lying. It is also good to demonstrate to those who say that the art is deceitful and false (as proof) so that they may learn: for what is true within it is entirely and truly so, and true *Sol* <term>Sol: Latin for "Sun." In alchemy, this refers to gold, the perfect metal, or the perfected state of matter.</term> is made or born through this art. For he says: In it all is truth and understanding in the highest degree, for the *Sol* produced through this art surpasses all natural Sun <note>original: "Solem"</note> in all properties, whether useful for medicine or other purposes.

<meta>Faint text at bottom appears to be bleed-through or ghosting from another page.</meta>

<summary>Hortulanus concludes his preface by stating that the Holy Spirit has allowed him to reveal the secrets of the philosophers. He then begins Chapter 1, asserting that alchemy is a true and certain art capable of producing a "Sun" (gold or medicine) that surpasses the natural variety.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet, moving from the introduction into the specific chapters that explain the text's famous axioms. The previous page established the author's credentials and the truth of alchemy; this page begins the technical and symbolic interpretation of Hermes' words regarding the division of the Stone and the origin of matter.</meta>

# That the Stone Shall Be Divided into Two Parts. Chapter 2.

Hermes says of the operation of the Stone <term>Lapis: Latin for "Stone." In alchemy, this refers to the Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals and perfecting the soul.</term> where he speaks: 
what is below is just like that which is above. And he says 
this because the Stone <note>original: "Lapis"</note> appears to be divided into 
two principal parts, namely, this mastery <term>Magisterium: The "Mastery" or "Great Work," referring to the entire process of alchemical transformation.</term> into the 
upper part which will ascend and into the lower part which 
remains floating below; and yet these two parts come 
together in virtue and power, and therefore he says: 
what is above is just like that which is below. This division, 
however, is necessary to bring about the wonders or 
miracles of one thing, namely, of the Stone. For the 
lower part is the earth <note>original: "Terra"</note> which is called a nurse <note>original: "Amma" — Likely a transcription of the Greek term for mother or nurse, corresponding to the Latin "nutrix" in the Emerald Tablet: "the earth is its nurse."</note> 
by Hermes <note>original: "Hermetem"</note>, and the upper part is the soul <note>original: "Anima"</note> 
which makes the whole Stone alive and stirs or awakens it; 
and for that reason, when the separation has occurred and the 
conjunction <term>Conjunction: The ritual or chemical reuniting of separated elements (often symbolized as a marriage) to create a unified, perfected substance.</term> is accomplished, many wonders are 
brought about in the secret work of nature.

---

# That the Stone Contains Within Itself the Four Elements. Chapter 3.

And as all things have come forth through an eternal counsel 
or providence: here he gives a clear example of how all things 
have come from one, namely, from a mass of matter <note>original: "Materia massa"</note> 
or a sphere through the Word—that is, through the providence or 
creation of an eternal being, that is, of Almighty God. Thus 
all things are created.

<summary>This page explains the famous alchemical maxim "as above, so below" by describing the division of the Philosopher's Stone into "earth" and "soul," and asserts that all matter originated from a single divine source.</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Emerald Tablet, Philosopher's Stone, Magisterium, Separation, Conjunction, Creation, Four Elements</keywords>

--- Page 214 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific chapter-by-chapter breakdown of the Emerald Tablet of Hermes. The previous page ended while explaining the division of the Stone into "above" and "below," identifying them as the Soul and the Earth/Nurse. This page transitions into Chapter 3, comparing the alchemical process to human birth and gestation.</meta>

# How our work is like that of a woman
## Chapter 3.

...it is near <note>original: "fere." This is a fragment of the sentence concluding the previous chapter.</note>. That they came together from this one thing, that is: out of a corrupted or perished mass through counsel and providence, which is through prayer and the miracle of God alone: as our Stone <term>Lapis: Latin for "Stone." In alchemy, this refers to the Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the Great Work.</term> was born and came forth from a virgin state, and has within it all elements which were created by God the Father: and through His miracles alone was our Stone born from it.

# That our Stone has a Father and a Mother,
## namely the Sun and the Moon. Chapter 4.

And just as we see in a natural way, that one animal gives birth to other animals like itself, so also does one Sun <note>original: "Sol"</note> unfailingly give birth to another, through the power of the multiplication of the aforementioned Stone, of which its Father is the Sun, the star of the philosophers <term>Astrum Philosophorum: Latin for "The Star of the Philosophers," referring to the spiritual or celestial influence that guides the alchemist's work and the internal light of the matter.</term> and because in all natural generation <term>Generation: The process of creation or giving birth to new life or matter.</term> there must be a birthing, a suitable and moist receptacle or mother with a simple uniformity to its Father, so also in this artificial <note>In the 17th century, "artificial" referred to something created through "Art"—specifically the Art of Alchemy—rather than being "fake."</note> birth it must be that the juice of its sperm or seed or seed-corn has a suitable receptacle or vessel to serve it. Here, take note: this is the silver of the philosophers <term>Argentum Philosophorum: Latin for "Silver of the Philosophers," often representing refined Mercury or the feminine principle of the Moon.</term> and thus follows that its mother is the Moon <note>original: "Luna"</note>.

# That the conjunction or joining together of the parts of the Stone is called the impregnation or pregnancy of our [Stone].
## Chapter 5.

Which, on the day when they [meet] each other in the conjunction <term>Conjunction: The alchemical stage where the male (Sun/Sulfur) and female (Moon/Mercury) principles are chemically and symbolically united.</term> of the Stone...

# Chapter: On the <unclear>Philosophical</unclear> Stone

Afterward, the Stone <term>Lapis: Latin for "Stone." In alchemy, this refers to the Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting long life.</term> is carried in the belly of the wind or the air, and this is the fourth element; [Hermes] says the wind has carried it in its belly. It is clear that the wind is the air, and the air is the life, and the life is the soul <note>original: "anima"</note>. I have already spoken before of the soul, that it makes the entire Stone alive; from which it follows that the air carries the whole Stone, and just as it carries and gives birth to the whole mastery <term>Magisterium: From the Latin for "mastery" or "office of a master." In alchemy, it refers to the completed Great Work or the process of achieving it.</term>, it then follows that it takes its nourishment from its mother, which is from the earth <note>original: "Terra"</note>. Upon this, the Philosopher <note>The "Philosopher" here refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the foundational figure of alchemy.</note> says: its nurse is the earth. For just as a child never reaches its proper age without the nourishment of its nurse, so also must this Stone never be brought to perfection without the fermentation of its earth—which fermentation is then called the nourishment. For thus it is born from a father with the help of its mother. These things—that is, the children—who are like their father: if they lack the long nourishment from their mother, they are similar in appearance and will retain their father's weight <gloss>weight</gloss> <note>original: "pondus"</note>.

### That the Stone is perfect when the Soul is Fixed in the body. Chapter 6.

Following this: This is a father of all perfection of the whole world. That is, in the sight of the Stone is the way to the end; and note, the Philosopher calls the operation a father of everything.

Telesma <note>A term from the Emerald Tablet, usually translated from the Greek as "perfection" or "completion." Here the author interprets it as the physical "sensuality" or "treasures" of the work.</note>, that is of all sensuality or of all treasures. Of the whole world, that is of our Stone <term>Lapis: The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical work.</term> as it is found in the whole world. Is it right to say this of the air? I tell you. Concerning that which the Philosopher <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy.</note> desires therein, should I teach you how the power of our matter <term>materia: The physical substance used to create the Stone.</term> of the Stone <term>Lapis</term> is perfect and complete? Simply: when it is transformed into its earth <term>Terra</term>; with this he says: its power is perfect—that is, complete and finished <note>original: "perfect und Complet"</note>—when it is transformed into its earth. That is the earth of the Stone.

But [as we] have established above, namely that the soul <term>anima</term> is changed into water and air, in which the whole fruit and the life of the Stone <term>Lapis</term> is transformed into earth <term>terra</term>. Namely, that the Stone <term>Lapis</term> be fixed <note>original: "figiert." In alchemy, "fixation" is the process of making a volatile, gaseous, or liquid substance stable and resistant to fire.</note>, so that the entire substance of the Stone unites with its sum; namely the earth, so that the entire Stone is then transformed into a ferment <term>Fermentum</term>. Just as in the making of bread, a little sourdough stirs and ferments a large heap of dough and thus transforms the entire substance of the dough into ferment <term>Fermentum</term>, so also does the Philosopher intend. That our Stone be called a "fermenter," so that the ferment <term>Fermentum</term> serves for the multiplication <term>multiplication: The stage of the work where the power and quantity of the Stone are increased.</term> of the Stone <term>Lapis</term>.

### On the Purification of the Stone, Chapter 3.

# How one should fix the Volatile Stone through and through. Chapter 9.
<note>original: "Lapidem Volatilem." In alchemy, "volatile" refers to a substance that easily vaporizes or escapes; "fixing" it means making it solid and resistant to heat.</note>

Accordingly, once one has thus dissolved this <term>Stone</term> and it is to be nourished with the oils that were extracted from the same <term>Stone</term> in the first operation—which is nothing other than the Water of the <term>Stone</term>—one must <unclear>cohobate</unclear> <note>original: "kohlen." Likely "cohobiren," the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid and pouring it back over the solid remains to concentrate the "essence."</note> and sublimate it until, through the power of **fermentation**, the Earth that was elevated with it descends entirely again from Heaven. Thus the Earth becomes spiritual and remains fixed and fluid. This is what the <term>Philosopher</term> <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy.</note> says: "And it ascends again to the earth," and so it receives the power of the things above through **Sublimation** <note>The process of turning a solid into vapor and back to solid to purify it.</note> and the things below through **Descent**. That is: that which is corporeal shall become spiritual through sublimating, and that which is spiritual shall become corporeal through descending.

# On the Benefit of the Art, the Effect, or the Power of the Stone. Chapter 10.

Thus you shall have the glory of the whole world; that is, when this <term>Stone</term> is thus prepared and composed, those who possess it have the glory of this world. From you all darkness shall be taken away—that is, all poverty and sickness—for the <term>Stone</term> thus prepared heals all diseases. This is the strength of all strengths, for compared to the strength of this <term>Stone</term> is...

## A figurative or concealed reminder and indication of the material of the Stone. Chapter 12.
<note>original: "verblühmte oder verdeckte erinnerung." In alchemical literature, "flowery" or "veiled" language was used to hide secrets from the uninitiated while signaling deeper truths to fellow "adepts."</note>

Therefore I have been named **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>Meaning "Thrice-Great Hermes," the legendary founder of alchemy.</note>, for I have then taught philosophy; the <term>composition</term> <note>The specific recipe or combination of elements.</note> of the <term>Stone</term> <note>original: "Lapidis."</note> consists in these words mentioned here, from which the Stone becomes and out of which it grows itself, so that no tongues <note>meaning no one who speaks or writes of this.</note> who come to this art should ever [fail to] be correctly mindful of its name. Afterwards, as if from Loranta <note>Possibly a corrupted reference to "Lull" (Ramon Llull) or another alchemical authority.</note>, where he says: There are three parts of the philosophy of the whole world; for whatever is in the world has <term>matter and form</term> <note>original: "materiam und formam." An Aristotelian concept that everything is made of physical substance (matter) and an organizing principle (form).</note> and is composed of the <term>elements</term> <note>Fire, Air, Earth, and Water.</note>. Therefore, there are countless parts of the world, all of which **Philosophy** divides into three noble parts: namely into the Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal [kingdoms]. Of all of these, or each in itself, the Philosopher has true knowledge in the <term>work of the Sun</term> <note>original: "wercke Solis." This refers to the creation of gold or the final perfection of the alchemical soul.</note>. Of this he says himself, that he has the three parts of the philosophy of the whole world, which are contained within this single Stone, namely in the **Philosophers' Trinity** <note>original: "Trio Philosophorum." This refers to the Stone containing the essence of all three kingdoms of life.</note>.

<meta>Continuing from the previous page's discussion on the division of philosophy, this concluding section of the text focuses on the specific composition and transformative process of the Philosophers' Stone, drawing on biblical metaphors of death and rebirth to explain the alchemical process.</meta>

[The Stone] is a single and infallible **Stone** <note>original: "Lapis"</note> that has three natures; namely, the 4 **elements** <note>original: "Elementa"</note> and three colors, specifically black, white, and red. It is called a small grain of wheat of the <term>leaven</term> <note>original: "fermenti." In alchemy, "leaven" or "ferment" is the substance added to a base metal to trigger its transformation into a higher state, much like yeast causes dough to rise.</note> which, unless it dies, remains alone; but if it dies—as was stated above—it comes forth and is <term>joined</term> <note>original: "Conjungiret."</note> in its <term>conjunction</term> <note>original: "Conjunction." A central alchemical stage where the "male" and "female" principles of matter are united.</note>, and it brings much fruit. Namely, as many times as the <term>operations</term> <note>original: "operationes." The specific laboratory steps or chemical procedures.</note> are completed. O you dear reader, if you knew the operations of the **Stone**, I have told you the truth; if you do not understand this, then nothing has been said to you. Now is fulfilled what I have said regarding the operation of the **Sun** <note>original: "Solis." In alchemical symbolic language, the Sun refers to gold or the perfected, masculine spirit of matter.</note>. Furthermore, what I have said is fulfilled concerning the operation of the **Stone**, which consists of three colors and three natures, as has been said, in an unconstrained way within **Philosophical Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio Philosophico." Not the common liquid metal, but a purified "first matter" that alchemists believed was the base of all metals.</note>.

# Compendium of the Divine Art
## Of Alchemy <note>original Latin: "Alchimiae"</note>

God, in the hall of Your throne,
Look down upon us in this valley, <note>A common metaphor for the earthly, mortal world as a "valley of tears."</note>
Upon everything that has human life,
In both spiritual and worldly estates,
Whether it be young or old,
Whether short or long in form,
From You, all things indeed have life,
As nature <unclear>clearly</unclear> testifies to us.
Yet the greatest part [of humanity] is so blind
That they do not find the best and the good,
Which is the Good and the Gold.
Through [the greed for] this, murderers, rogues, and thieves are made;
It is common throughout the whole world,
Even emperors and kings desire it,
As do counts and the free-born, <note>Refers to the "Freiherren" or free nobility.</note>
And they would gladly know what that might be,
Whoever might desire such a thing.
Grant us, Lord Jesus Christ,
That we may find that very thing
Upon which the whole world is founded,
Namely, the Philosopher's Stone, <note>The legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life; here treated as a divine gift.</note>
Which, <unclear>in total</unclear>, <note>original Latin: "In Summa"</note> is not common! <note>Meaning it is rare, precious, or not easily understood by the masses.</note>

But where one happens to lose it,
It may well rob him of a hundred talers; <note>The *Thaler* was a large silver coin used throughout Europe; the author warns that alchemy is an expensive pursuit that can lead to ruin.</note>
Yet out of a true desire,
Through the one who is called **Mercury**, <note>original: "Mercurius"</note>
The great master in all the world,
How many people does he deprive of their money!
Yet one must always return to it,
For indeed everyone desires to have him.
Whom you should bake, steam, boil, and roast,
Then he, in turn, shall comfort you,
For then he is called the very equal of silver,
Because of which he deceives both poor and rich.
In **Latin** <note>original: "Zu latin"</note> one calls this **coagulation**, <note>original: "Coaguliren." This refers to the alchemical process of "fixing" a liquid substance into a solid state.</note>
With which he leads many astray,
Because they look upon common tin,
And thus they go against the current.
Many a servant and lord truly believes
They shall require no more labor,
Save that they might enjoy his fruit;
But then it first smells of sin. <note>Likely a reference to the foul odors produced during alchemical heating, often compared to sulfur or corruption.</note>
It is such a fruit indeed,
That one seldom becomes aware of it.
But that he might perform some use here,
It happens through the "cutting of blood." <note>This likely refers to a specific stage of the "Great Work" where the "red" state or "tincture" is achieved.</note>
Therefore, consider this well beforehand,
So that you may hold him rightly,
And **cut off his wings**,
So that he has no more feet. <note>A classic alchemical allegory: "Fixing" Mercury (quicksilver) is described as clipping its wings so the volatile spirit cannot "fly away" or evaporate during heating.</note>

That he now makes a **mercantile work** <note>original: *kauffwerck*. This refers to the business of alchemy or the "trade" of making gold, often used by authors to contrast the common greed for money with the true spiritual "Art."</note> ring out,
I fear he still twists many who went before,
From the states he can also maintain the deed,
Therefore the way is withheld from us,
But for us it is still the very best,
How one leaves all his limbs to him,
And places him into a simple vessel,
And burns out the work with **fire**, <note>original: *feur*. Fire is the primary tool of the alchemist, used to "purge" the impurities from the matter.</note>
So that he can make himself visible;
Therefore he will cause laughter
When one aims for his heart,
Then he shows his **navel rods** <note>original: *Nabel Ruthen*. This likely refers to the central point of the matter or the "umbilical" connection between the material substance and its spiritual source.</note>
And he lies within; he requires the bold one.
He had eyes with a luster like a torch,
So he eats very little evermore,
So that I regurgitate the grass I once devoured, <note>A common alchemical image where the "Green Lion" or the dragon consumes matter and then "returns" it in a purified state.</note>
When this fire, while it can **kindle**, <note>original: *Zundern*.</note>
Therefore let everyone beware;
He will also bend his horn in such a way
That he flies through all seals,
He pulls the horse away from its place,
For he who has cared for him well,
Has not done so for him in vain;
He adorns himself kindly through his **Art**, <note>original: *Kunst*; specifically the Alchemical Art.</note>
Giving him as a reward a **golden brain**, <note>original: *gulden Gehirn*. This represents the completion of the Great Work, where the alchemist's own intellect is "transmuted" into a state of divine wisdom or "gold."</note>
No one can drive out that joy,
For no teaching comes from it,
Whoever wants to be content with him,
Although the **Art** and the day
Many a person will say such things,

<meta>This page transitions from the general theoretical framework of the Rosarium and Arnaldus de Villa Nova to a first-person allegorical poem. The speaker assumes the persona of a "King" (likely representing the perfected Gold or the Philosopher's Stone) and introduces the central alchemical figure of the serpent. This follows the previous page's discussion of the "First Matter" by illustrating its transformation from common metals into a singular, living force.</meta>

A King I will give myself to be named here,
According to my word, one should live here,
God has created seven metals <note>In alchemy, the seven metals correspond to the seven celestial bodies: Gold (Sun), Silver (Moon), Copper (Venus), Iron (Mars), Tin (Jupiter), Lead (Saturn), and Quicksilver (Mercury).</note>,
None of which please me,
Though they are all to be used,
Through their governance they are revealed,
That the star is enclosed within them <note>The "star" often refers to the internal "signature" or the celestial influence trapped within terrestrial matter.</note>,
Through inclination one acquires skill,
Of which we truly have many blessed intentions,
O God my Lord, we are Your children,
What lies secretly within the metals,
Was that I must set the **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note> free,
And not lock all metals upon him,
In the hope I would enjoy her great grace <note>The "grace" of the material's transformation.</note>,
Now hear my report here:
With [ordinary] metals it may not happen,
To work by **Particulars** <note>original: "Particular machen." "Particular" work refers to transmuting a specific amount of base metal into a higher one, as opposed to the "Universal" work which creates the Stone that can transform any quantity.</note> is rather in vain,
But observe for me this wondrous figure,
A serpent slithers from a hole,
Then my heart becomes full of joy,
I ventured with many insights <note>original: "Ein hellen," likely referring to illuminations or helpers.</note>,
[And] with her came into great distress,
I soon saw my harm approaching me,
It seemed to me a great army
Of serpents came to her aid,
And it took away a small pious [man],
How often I sat up because of her,
Yet saw no wonder in her again,
What seemed to me so wondrously formed,
The likes of which wonder was seldom found before.

The serpent has become so strong against me,
If she were [taken] into hand once more,
Therefore I must suffer great impatience,
That my sword <note>The "sword" in alchemy often symbolizes the "sharp" solvent or the heat of the fire used to divide and penetrate matter.</note> would not cut the serpent;
In my weapons there must also be diligence,
Otherwise she would have no way of thinking,
Yet she made the truth known to me,
And for honor's sake called me Master,
Who was the radiance of a King or a State;
God willed that we all succeed.
Still more the worm <note>original: "Wurm," a common alchemical synonym for the serpent or dragon representing the volatile primal matter.</note> spoke, I say:
"I have a brother and a sister with me,
With whom one may completely extinguish me;
Otherwise no one, believe me, has understanding,
When one gives me a fine **Individuum** <term>Individuum: Referring here to the specific, indivisible essence or the singular "thing" that constitutes the starting material.</term>,
The brother with my sister.
Through them [the matter] must be minutely separated,
Must surely die through them, believe [me];
They are made living through me,
And all three are brought into one.
This becomes a new thing to me;
Blessed is he who so overcomes me."

That we reach a conclusion;

# BY THE MOST ILLUSTRIOUS

## Prince and Lord Frederick, Count Palatine of the Rhine, Arch-Steward <term>Arch-Steward: A high-ranking ceremonial office within the Holy Roman Empire, traditionally held by the Electors of the Palatinate.</term> of the Holy Roman Empire, Prince-Elector, and Duke of Bavaria, my most Merciful Lord.

---

In the name of the Lord. Let there be made one thing or substance, yet first composed and mixed from two in such a way that the smallest part of one is joined with the smallest part of the other without separation forever: finally, let the joined matter be **fixed** <term>fixed: The process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to heat.</term> and **dissolved** again and again, until from it emerges a **meltable medicine**, malleable, refined, spiritual, corporeal, thin, dense, light, and heavy. This medicine, when placed over the fire, melts like a metal before it flees <note>original: "ante fugam," a term used when a substance evaporates or "escapes" the crucible before it can be transformed.</note> and is mixed with all metals through their smallest parts, penetrating them in such a way as if it were formed of their own substance, so that it can never be separated from them by any craft, because they have now become one thing. Moreover, it will convert all things with which it has a kinship <note>original: "Simbolum," referring to a shared chemical or spiritual affinity between substances.</note> into its own nature. When, however, a stronger mixing and preparation precedes, an even more abundant **transmutation** follows, since this depends on the mixing and follows it just as a cause follows its effect.

Therefore, every effort must be made so that this result follows, which will happen without sublimation <note>original: "sublimatione," the process of heating a substance until it becomes vapor and then solidifies again.</note> since it is itself a medium, like a certain transition, causing such a conjunction; however, it is necessary to cook the sublimated matter for a long time...
